12 Powdered Gravy Mixes, Ranked Worst To Best

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

As most pro home chefs will tell you, making gravy from scratch isn't exactly rocket science. With the help of a little flour, butter, and broth, you could be on your way to a delicious gravy in just a few minutes. Still, there may be times when you simply aren't feeling up to it — whether you've had a rough week or are drowning in to-dos, packets of powdered gravy mix can prove a simple, flavorful fix.

Even so, the kind of store-bought gravy mix you choose matters — after all, anything you're planning to drown your meat and taters in had better be good. That's why I decided to make it my mission to test some of the most popular powdered gravy options in my area in hopes of offering my honest opinion about which truly deserves a spot in your kitchen

In the upcoming post, I'm giving a complete rundown of every store-bought powdered gravy mix I could find, including the pricing, size, and taste of each. Bear in mind that the details mentioned in this article are subject to change and that the gravy packet selection featured here may not be reflective of what's available in your area. With that, join me as I prepare, taste, and rank 12 store-bought powdered gravy mixes from worst to best.

Prices are as of the date of publication and may vary based on region.

Great Value Chicken Gravy Mix is a no-frills Walmart-branded product that is, surprisingly, one of the costlier options on the list when you consider its price per unit. Though tagged at only 92 cents per packet, each pack contains .87 ounces, meaning you'll pay roughly $1.06 per ounce of powdered gravy.

Great Value Chicken Gravy Mix is prepared using the standard 1 cup of water per 1 packet of mix, and its ingredients include monosodium glutamate (otherwise known as "MSG"), which may pose a problem for gravy lovers who may be sensitive to the ingredient. I tore open the packet, and its unappetizing, artificial smell hit me right away. Once cooked, the mixture turned creamy and thick, but its color seemed unnatural, especially when compared to homemade. I gave it a hesitant taste, and nope, not good — Great Value Chicken Gravy Mix tasted like stale chicken and literally made me gag.

Would I recommend purchasing Great Value Chicken Gravy Mix? No way. Not only does its bright yellow color not resemble any chicken gravy I've ever made, but the taste was barely tolerable. I'm sure you could try improving this store-bought gravy with Worcestershire sauce, but I say, if it's convenience you seek, there are other options more deserving of your coin than this one.

I hate to have to rank yet another Great Value powdered gravy mix so close to the bottom of the list, but it simply cannot be helped. Great Value Turkey Gravy Mix is a bit cheaper than its chicken-flavored counterpart — expect to pay 78 cents per .87 ounces, which works out to around 89.7 cents per ounce of powdered gravy. Great Value Turkey Gravy Mix doesn't contain the MSG that the chicken flavor includes, but that doesn't mean I recommend purchasing it — like the former, it has a weird smell and taste that I simply can't get past.

I prepared Great Value Turkey Gravy Mix according to the package directions and watched it bubble and simmer on the stove. The mix eventually turned the typical light brown color normally associated with turkey gravy and had an uber-thick consistency. I let it cool slightly before giving it a quick taste — sadly, it had the same stomach-churning qualities as the previous flavor. Great Value Turkey Gravy Mix tasted woefully artificial; in fact, the additives or chemicals present in the gravy were just about the only thing I could taste. Needless to say, I would never dish up Great Value Turkey Gravy Mix to guests at Thanksgiving — you'd be better off leaving not-so-Great Value picks like this one on the shelf.

Before offering my opinion here, I have to be honest and say I'm not a person who prepares peppered gravy often. That said, I'm willing to bet Southeastern Mills Peppered Gravy Mix doesn't quite encapsulate its classic flavor the way other options do.

Southeastern Mills Peppered Gravy Mix costs $1.22 per 2.75 ounces, with a unit price of 44.4 cents per ounce. As is typical for many peppered gravy mixes on the market, this packet contains a little more powdered gravy mix per pack than other varieties and flavors. Its preparation requires more water, too — you'll need 1 ¾ cups this time around versus the usual 1 cup.

Southeastern Mills Peppered Gravy Mix tasted annoyingly bland, with a flavor very similar to what you'd get if you added black pepper to plain cream. Though thick, it had no richness of flavor, and I couldn't imagine it doing much to elevate anyone's sausage gravy. I flipped the packet over and was surprised by what I saw on the ingredient list: Palm oil and modified food starch were the first ingredients mentioned, which is hardly a good way to start a high-quality gravy, if you ask me. As I said, I am by no means an expert in peppered gravy, but based on first impressions alone, I'd say skip this brand and opt for other, richer, more flavorful brands on the market.

If you're surprised to see McCormick Brown Gravy Mix ranked lower on the list, join the club. I, for one, adore brown gravy, and with McCormick being one of the most popular gravy and seasoning brands on the market, I figured I'd be in for a real treat. Expect to pay 97 cents for .87 ounces of it, making each ounce cost roughly $1.12.

When cooking up McCormick Brown Gravy Mix, I couldn't help but feel excited — its texture was thick and glossy, and the gravy had a deep brown hue that just screamed umami beef. It even had the savory, enticing aroma of a succulent pot roast, so imagine my surprise when I discovered the gravy tastes, quite literally, like nothing.

That's right — McCormick Brown Gravy Mix features a flavor so weak I could barely taste anything at all. To think McCormick Brown Gravy Mix smelled and looked so great without delivering flavor even half of what was teased is mind-boggling. Needless to say, McCormick Brown Gravy Mix isn't worth the buy — I'd recommend picking up some beef broth and making your own simple brown gravy or choosing one of the higher-ranking varieties over this one.

Though not the best out there, Amazon Grocery Brown Gravy Mix is a dark horse in terms of taste and performance. The gravy comes sold in an .87-ounce pack and was priced at 83 cents, about 95 cents per ounce. It contains artificial ingredients along with a host of additives, but hey, if that kinda stuff doesn't bother you, feel free to ignore those stats.

I went into my tasting of Amazon Grocery Brown Gravy Mix with low expectations — almost every Amazon Grocery item I've sampled in the past left me underwhelmed. Much to my amazement, Amazon Grocery Brown Gravy Mix held its own in terms of consistency and aroma — it was a bit thinner than a few other options on the list, yes, but still managed to be just thick enough. I also found its glossy deep brown appearance mesmerizing, and its smell rich and meaty, just the way a good brown gravy should be.

Going in for a slurp, I didn't walk away disappointed — though the flavor was nowhere near as potent as its aroma, it was leaps and bounds better than McCormick. I could clearly taste its beefy notes, and its umami depth was decent overall. One thing I will say is that Amazon Grocery Brown Gravy Mix tastes slightly watered down; still, I think it will get you by if you're looking for a tasty, inexpensive brown gravy to crown your mashers.

Pioneer Peppered Gravy Mix is pretty good; though not a peppered gravy kinda gal, I have an appreciation for this brand and would recommend it over Southeastern Mills any day. This mix comes in a 2.75-ounce packet, only this time, you'll need to add 2 cups of water instead of the 1 ¾ cups Southeastern Mills requires. Each packet costs $1.33, meaning you'll cop this powdered peppered gravy for about 48.4 cents per ounce.

When cooking up Pioneer Peppered Gravy Mix, the first thing I noticed was its golden hue. The yellowish tinge of the Pioneer-branded gravy made it more attractive than the stark white presentation of Southeastern Mills. It was just as thick as the former brand, if not thicker, though whether or not its flavor would prove better was yet to be seen.

So, how'd Pioneer Peppered Gravy Mix taste? Though I still consider its flavor mundane compared to traditional chicken or brown gravies, Pioneer Peppered Gravy Mix had more depth than Southeastern Mills. It's not listed in the ingredients, but there seemed to be a hint of butter flavor in the mixture — this deepened the taste and added complexity. All in all, if I were to grab a packet of peppered gravy for breakfast, this is the brand I'd get — not only does it look better, but its taste is pretty decent, too.

McCormick Turkey Gravy Seasoning Mix wasn't too bad — it isn't something I'd choose over homemade gravy for Thanksgiving dinner, but it's good if you're in a pinch and want something cheap. The gravy comes sold in a .87-ounce packet for $1.63, and while this might not sound super expensive, it's actually a little pricier than others, at $1.87 per ounce.

McCormick Turkey Gravy Seasoning Mix yields a light brown color I found fitting for turkey gravy — it certainly looked the part, featuring a medium-thick consistency and glossy appearance. McCormick Turkey Gravy Seasoning Mix has a poultry-like flavor that's pleasing but leans more chicken than turkey in my opinion. Nevertheless, I consider this turkey gravy option to be one of the punchier, more robust picks out there, and though I don't necessarily consider it holiday worthy, it might be good to have a packet or two in your pantry for days when you're low on time.

Fire & Smoke Society Big Sunday Brown Gravy Mix is a super unique option, and though I probably wouldn't buy it again, you might consider trying it if you're looking to sample something outside of the norm. The packaging here has the kind of aesthetic that makes you think it's fancy by looks alone — its price also stands out, at $1.72 per 1-ounce pack, working out to $1.72 per ounce. A quick scan of the ingredients reveals several surprises — though not a clean ingredient pick by any means, some additions — like cumin, chili powder, and even coffee — left me curious as to what kind of flavor I was in for.

I prepared the gravy as per its 1 cup to 1 packet instructions and immediately noticed a difference in its texture compared to the others. Fire & Smoke Society Big Sunday Brown Gravy Mix yields a thinner, silkier texture than most. Its aroma was totally unexpected, as it was more akin to grill spices than savory beef. After tasting Fire & Smoke Society's Big Sunday Brown Gravy Mix, I found myself torn — the taste wasn't gross, but didn't encompass the type of flavor I expect from a classic brown gravy.

Is Fire & Smoke Society Big Sunday Brown Gravy Mix good? Yes! Would I buy it again? Probably not. Blame it on my traditional taste buds, but for me, this powdered gravy mix is just OK.

Is there a difference between country and peppered gravy? Apparently, there is, and as it stands, I prefer the former. Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning was a breath of fresh air flavor-wise, but I suppose that isn't surprising given I love practically anything that the Kinder brand produces. Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning was priced at $1.72 per 1.9 ounces at my local Walmart, working out to about 90.5 cents per ounce.

Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning was truly phenomenal in terms of taste and texture. First and foremost, the gravy recommends preparing it with 2 cups of milk, which automatically gave it a richer, sweeter finish. Once cooked, the gravy was thick and featured a stark white appearance flecked with tons of contrasting black pepper throughout.

Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning tastes almost identical to creamy, buttery, garlicky mashed potatoes, no spuds needed. I'd imagine this would taste incredible paired with creamy taters, and I wouldn't hesitate to dish it up as a turkey topper during the holidays. As if things couldn't get better, Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning uses fairly clean ingredients, with nowhere near as much of the ick featured in other powdered gravy mixes on the list. With all things considered, Kinder's Classic Country Gravy Dry Seasoning is one of my favorite powdered gravy mixes on the market.

Simply Organic Roasted Chicken Gravy Seasoning Mix costs $1.72 for .85 ounces making it one of the most expensive powdered gravy mixes on the market at $2.02 per ounce. It's the only gluten-free organic chicken option on the list, and I honestly didn't expect it to taste as good as it did. Not that I have anything against organic foods — I actually love them and find the use of minimal ingredients refreshing. Still, in the world of gravy, I guess I just didn't expect a natural option like this one to hold its own when pitted against others containing various flavor-boosting additives. Thankfully, this humble pick from Simply Organic proved me wrong.

Simply Organic chicken gravy calls for the standard 1 cup of gravy packet seasoning per 1 cup of water. As it cooked, it began to take on a sauce-like consistency that was thick, glossy, and light brown. I gave it a taste and was floored — this organic pick boasted a flavor way better than any other store-bought chicken gravy I've tried.

Simply Organic chicken gravy had a bold, savory, and garlicky flavor beautifully enhanced with prominent notes of celery seed. Thus, though expensive, you can rest assured that your money is going to good use — Simply Organic Roasted Chicken Gravy Seasoning Mix is a phenomenal replacement for homemade when you don't feel like whipping up chicken gravy on your own.

Simply Organic Brown Gravy Seasoning Mix is the only organic brown gravy on the list, and let me tell you, it did not disappoint. Unlike its chicken-flavored predecessor, Simply Organic Brown Gravy Seasoning Mix is slightly cheaper, at 1 ounce for $1.72 ( $1.72 per ounce). Being the organic grab that it is, expect Simply Organic brown gravy to be non-GMO and free of artificial colors, flavors, and ingredients. One of its best attributes is its use of cornstarch rather than flour, as this makes it a great option for people looking to ditch wheat.

I was delighted to find Simply Organic Brown Gravy Seasoning Mix packing so much flavor — after the previous brown gravy duds, I didn't have much hope that there was a truly good packaged option out there. In truth, Simply Organic's brown gravy is one of the most flavorful in its class — it was one of the few to truly capture the beefy, oniony savor I come to expect from a good homemade brown gravy.

One thing to note is that Simply Organic brown gravy has a medium consistency and a color a lot lighter than others in its category. Still, Simply Organic Brown Gravy Seasoning Mix would make the perfect companion to roast beef or over creamy mashed potatoes, and I doubt anyone would be able to detect the difference between it and homemade.

Yellowstone Jalapeño Gravy is unlike any other gravy I've tried — I had never sampled jalapeño gravy before, nor did I know it existed. Now that it's on my radar, in my cabinet it will stay. It's really that good, even if it is $2.50 per 2.82-ounce packet (89 cents per ounce)!

Yellowstone Jalapeño Gravy contains no artificial flavors, which to me is pretty impressive. Though not gluten-free like the aforementioned Simply Organic picks, it does at least contain fewer additives while still managing to achieve unbelievably thick results. You only need to use 2 cups of water per packet to cash in on this irresistibly luscious golden gravy; its texture was so rich and smooth my mouth watered just at the sight of it.

Aside from its tantalizing consistency, Yellowstone Jalapeño Gravy tastes incredible — I'd describe its flavor as similar to high-quality jalapeño nacho cheese, but with less emphasis on the cheese. It's quite spicy, and though not unbearably so, still isn't something I'd recommend offering to kids. All in all, Yellowstone Jalapeño Gravy is an amazing option I'd buy over and over again — try it on fried chicken cutlets, potatoes, stuffing, or hot sandwiches. Yum!

The 12 powdered gravy mixes selected for this article were chosen based on availability in my area, with most sourced from Walmart and Amazon. Each gravy mix was ranked primarily based on texture and flavor, although other factors, like ingredients and pricing, were considered. As always, pricing and availability are subject to change.

Google Warns North Korean Hackers Are Poisoning AI Tools to Drain Crypto Firms

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Home » Crypto Market News » Google Warns North Korean Hackers Are Poisoning AI Tools to Drain Crypto Firms

State-backed hackers are using fake recruiters and tampered Claude Code settings to breach crypto networks.

Cyberattacks against the cryptocurrency industry have crossed a dangerous threshold as threat actors shift from basic AI prompts to autonomous AI agents capable of running intrusions from start to finish.

According to the Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) and Mandiant report, state-backed hackers are actively targeting crypto firms in North America and Europe by turning popular AI developer tools into malware delivery traps.

Researchers have linked a suspected North Korean hacking campaign to attacks on cryptocurrency companies. The group, known as Midnight Neptune, uses fake recruiter profiles and tampered software settings to break into company networks.

The attackers approach targets on professional networking platforms, posing as recruiters with AI-generated profile photos.

They then invite victims to fake online meetings, where simulated audio or video problems prompt them to run scripts to fix the issue. This social engineering trick, known as ClickFix, gets victims to execute malicious commands themselves.

Once inside a company’s network, the attackers tamper with its internal code repositories, modifying the configuration file used by Anthropic’s Claude Code.

The next time a developer launches Claude Code, the altered settings silently download a backdoor called SOMBERMEME.

The attackers also create GitHub Personal Access Tokens disguised as Microsoft Copilot to make their activity look like normal developer traffic.

The report also revealed a major advance in automated hacking that puts open-source Web3 infrastructure at risk.

Google researchers discovered an active tool called exploit_agent on an exposed testing server. It uses large language models (LLMs) to compare software code before and after security updates, identify newly patched vulnerabilities, write code to exploit them, and launch attacks — all without human involvement.

That puts open-source protocols at risk. Since code updates are publicly available, hackers can spot newly fixed vulnerabilities and exploit them before operators have a chance to install the patches. Even a brief delay can leave networks vulnerable.

Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) reported that disclosed vulnerabilities and exploits nearly doubled between January and August 2026.

Exploited zero-day vulnerabilities also rose from an average of eight per month in 2025 to 11 in 2026, reaching 22 in August alone.

Beyond traditional cyberattacks, Mandiant warns of a growing threat called LLMJacking. Criminals hijack corporate cloud accounts, steal AI computing power, and resell it through underground marketplaces known as LLM Mixers. The resulting fraud can exceed $100,000 in a single day and reach $1 million per victim.

Meanwhile, influence networks use more than 100,000 fake social media accounts to spread misinformation, fuel token hype, and trigger artificial market panic.

To counter these threats, Google is deploying AI security tools such as Big Sleep and CodeMender to find and fix software vulnerabilities before hackers exploit them.

The GTIG report highlights several security risks for the crypto industry.

Companies using AI coding tools should check their code repositories for unauthorized commands and keep third-party access tokens secure.

They also need to install software updates quickly, as AI-powered hackers can turn newly discovered vulnerabilities into working attacks.

Employees should never follow unexpected browser or video-call instructions asking them to copy and run computer commands.

Investors, meanwhile, should be cautious of sudden waves of online hype or panic, which fake social media accounts can create at scale.

Latest findings point to fuel supply, not Blue Origin’s engines, in loss of Amazon Leo satellites

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

by Todd Bishop on Nov 18, 2026 at 10:01 amNovember 18, 2026 at 10:08 am

Blue Origin’s engines look like they’re off the hook, at least for now.

The two BE-4 engines Blue Origin makes for United Launch Alliance’s Vulcan rocket weren’t to blame for the low thrust in Sunday’s failed launch, according to preliminary findings released Wednesday. Instead, it appears the engines didn’t get enough fuel from the rocket, ULA said.

In its latest update on the investigation, ULA said it had completed an initial integrated review of flight data. Preliminary data indicate the first stage’s low thrust came from “inadequate fuel supply from the vehicle to the BE-4 engines, not from the performance of the engines,” ULA said, adding that work remains to determine the cause.

On Tuesday, ULA said early data suggested the first stage underperformed just after liftoff, leaving the rocket too low and too slow to put 40 of Amazon’s Leo internet satellites into orbit. It didn’t say then whether the engines played a role.

The BE-4s are made by Jeff Bezos’ Kent, Wash.-based Blue Origin. Blue Origin CEO Dave Limp said Wednesday on X that he was proud of the BE-4 team for its “rapid response and support of ULA’s investigation” and that the company looks forward to Vulcan’s return to flight.

The finding matters for Blue Origin beyond Vulcan. Its own New Glenn rocket uses seven BE-4s on its first stage, and Amazon has 24 New Glenn launches reserved for Leo.

ULA repeated that Vulcan’s second stage appears to have started normally and that its RL10 engines operated as planned but couldn’t make up for the earlier loss of performance. The six Northrop Grumman solid rocket boosters, the source of problems on two earlier Vulcan flights, appear to have performed normally, ULA said.

The Federal Aviation Administration is overseeing the ULA-led investigation. Amazon’s next Leo launch is an Ariane 6 mission carrying 36 satellites, targeted for no earlier than Nov. 23 from French Guiana.

ULA said it will share more as the investigation continues.

Colts CB Justin Walley earns AFC Defensive Player of the Week honors after momentous strip-sack

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

On the opening snap of the second half against the Miami Dolphins, Indianapolis Colts cornerback Justin Walley disguised a nickel blitz to come screaming off the edge against an empty pocket. Dolphins quarterback Kyle McCord tried to escape, but Walley closed in to lay a hit before McCord had enough time to react. Walley punched the football out of his grasp for a strip-sack and sent the leather tumbling into the end zone.

Defensive tackle Adetomiwa Adebawore hustled to recover the fumble for a touchdown, giving Indianapolis a defensive score that ultimately proved to be the winning play in its 24-22 victory over Miami inside Lucas Oil Stadium. Walley admitted as soon as defensive coordinator Lou Anarumo dialed up the playcall, he knew it was his moment to wreak havoc in the backfield and terminate Miami's drive just seconds after it got started.

The momentum-shifting play earned Walley AFC Defensive Player of the Week honors, the first league-wide recognition of his NFL career. Walley finished with five tackles, including two tackles for loss, two passes defensed and a pivotal strip-sack. His standout performance makes him the first defensive back to reach that statline in a single NFL game since at least 1993.

Indianapolis managed to score just three points from its offense in the second half, letting a comfortable two-possession advantage shrink to two points as Miami mounted a rally. Walley's strip-sack gave the Colts enough cushion to withstand Miami's late push. The 24-year-old posted a 91.2 Pro Football Focus defensive grade in Week 10, which was the highest among all cornerbacks and third-best among all NFL players. He made life difficult for McCord in his first NFL start, giving up just three catches on six targets for a measly 13 yards.

Through 10 games, Walley has collected 40 tackles, including three tackles for loss, two sacks, three forced fumbles, and five pass break ups. His 22 defensive stops rank second among all NFL cornerbacks this season.

The Colts selected Walley in the third round with the 80th overall pick in the 2025 NFL Draft out of Minnesota, with intentions to add a young, versatile nickel to their secondary. His NFL introduction didn't go according to plan, because Walley missed his entire rookie season after suffering a torn ACL during a joint-practice with the Baltimore Ravens in training camp, delaying an early opportunity to establish himself on the field.

For a player who missed his rookie campaign, the recognition represents the journey it took to return to the gridiron. Walley is the fourth Colts cornerback to earn AFC Defensive Player of the Week honors over the past decade, joining Kenny Moore II (twice), Jaylon Jones and Xavier Rhodes.

The Colts (3-7) needed someone on the defensive side of the ball to make a play and halt the Dolphins' first drive of the second half. Adebawore deserves credit for recovering the football, but Walley's fingerprints knocked the football loose into the blue turf for the fumble recovery touchdown.

Grand Theft Auto 6 Performance Is Mostly Stable, Except On Xbox Series S Where It Can Hit Sub-20FPS

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

With Grand Theft Auto 6âs world being as big as it is and current consoles being six years old, thereâs naturally a bit of worry about how the game performs on PS5 and Xbox Series X/S. Our reviewer played the game on a PS5 Pro, considered to be the most powerful console on the market right now, and said that while it runs at 30 frames-per-second most of the time, there were still some noticeable frame drops in crowded areas.

But if you havenât bought a PS5 Pro, what can you expect from GTA 6âs performance? Well, the folks over at Digital Foundry have done what they do best and created some lengthy technical breakdowns of the game, including stress testing it in explosive situations.

According to their video, the PS5 Pro tends to hold a pretty solid 30fps, unless you go around making a mess by blowing up a lot of cars at once; in those moments Digital Foundry saw the game dip to around 24fps at its lowest. The base PS5, however, seems to struggle to maintain 30fps in the best of cases, and if you decide to cause a lot of on-screen destruction, those dips are actually sustained until you leave an area.Â

On the Xbox side, the Xbox Series X seems to share the same relative stability as the PS5 Pro, with the same frame drops during car chases, crowded scenes, or firefights when things get busier on screen. The big question is how the Xbox Series S, which was originally released in 2020 as a cheaper, lower-powered alternative to the bougie, more expensive consoles of the year, handles GTA 6. According to Digital Foundry, there are some details that are less prevalent or rendered with lower-res textures, and the resolution is lower, coming in at 720p compared to the 1440p of Series X.

The DF video has a number of comparison shots demonstrating how a lot of small details have been downgraded on Series S, but the real difference is that the framerate is, unsurprisingly, struggling the most on the less powerful Xbox. Digital Foundry reports that while 30fps is the target, even the most “typical” play can affect framerate, with standard combat encounters and car chases dropping it to around 25 and the more demanding carnage bringing it down to 20. There’s even a few seconds in the video where it gets down to 18 and 19fps during an explosive shootout.

Ultimately, if youâre hoping for the smoothest possible experience, the PS5 Pro and Xbox Series X are the unsurprising winners here. A PC version of the game is rumored to launch sometime next year, and I imagine thatâs where folks with high-end builds are going to be able to get smoother, 60fps framerates as Rockstar apparently has no plans to add a Performance Mode to GTA6 on consoles. If youâre running a Series S, maybe try not to cause too much mayhem along the way for best results.Â

Though the Xbox Series S provided a more affordable way for people to jump into the next generation of gaming consoles in 2020, as time has gone on and games have gotten more demanding on the hardware, developers have expressed frustration with having to make their games compatible with the device. Tekken director Katsuhiro Harada described it as akin to âhaving to do extra work for a slightly weaker child.â

Jared Padalecki Officially Addresses His 'Fire Country' Spin-Off's Future [Exclusive]

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Jeffrey is a freelance features writer at Collider. He is an MPA-accredited entertainment journalist and a Tomatometer-approved critic based in the LA area. He graduated from the University of Texas at Austin with a Bachelor of Science in Radio, TV, & Film and a Bachelor of Arts in Theater.

Two years ago, Jared Padalecki debuted on Fire Country in an exciting three-episode arc as Camden Casey, the new training captain for Bode Leone (Max Thieriot) when Bode became a newly minted firefighter-in-training. During Padalecki's run, Camden ran into some conflicts with Bode and his father, Battalion Chief Vincent Leone (Billy Burke), over Bode not keeping his emotions in check in the field. Camden hails from Southern California and has a very different personality and firefighting style from the residents of Edgewater, bringing some nice dramatic tension to the series.

Around the time Padalecki was set to debut in Fire Country Season 3, he also extended a first-look deal with CBS, which would explore a potential spin-off for Camden that would take viewers out of Edgewater and to the Southern California coast. Tentatively titled Fire Country: Surfside, the series looked like an exciting prospect. Unfortunately, it appears that Padalecki is moving on to a new show that likely lowers the chances of a Camden Casey series coming to fruition.

It now looks like the Supernatural veteran will be trading in his firefighter helmet for some medical scrubs in his next project. Deadline reported this month that Padalecki is re-teaming with his Walker executive producer Anna Fricke for a new Texas-based medical drama for CBS. The currently untitled medical series was previously set up for development in January, and now it’s gearing up for a new development/writers' room. Although the series hasn't received an official order, Deadline notes that CBS is leaning heavily into "off-cycle orders" for shows that have undergone lengthy development or writers' rooms.

Four of the largest communities on the planet, and four completely different reasons to belong to one. Here is yours, what it would give you, and the argument you would be having within a week.

You belong to the fandom that is really about comfort. Hogwarts works on people because it is a place rather than a plot, and the pull is not the wizard war so much as the corridors, the food, the weather and the sense that somebody is expecting you.

You belong to the deepest fandom on this list, the one where somebody has always read further than you and is quietly delighted to say so. Middle-earth rewards patience more than any other world in popular culture.

You belong to the loudest and largest tent of the four, the one where three generations are all watching the same thing and none of them agree about any of it. That disagreement is not a flaw in this fandom, it is the main activity.

You belong to the thinking fandom, the one that turns up for the argument rather than the explosion. Trek has always been a series of ethical problems in space, and the people who love it love being asked what they would have done.

The subject matter is near and dear to Padalecki, as the show would be set in his home state of Texas and focus on rural medicine at smaller, more remote hospitals, in contrast to other medical dramas such as The Pitt, which are typically set in major urban centers. Unfortunately, Deadline also noted there's been no movement on Fire Country: Surfside, or a return to Fire Country for the actor. Fire Country's original showrunner, Tia Napolitano, previously left the door open for the actor to return, but Napolitano exited the series with the fourth season's conclusion, which likely lowers the chances for Padalecki reprising the role.

Speaking with Collider's Tania Hussein for a profile, Padalecki exclusively addressed the future of Fire Country: Surfside. The good news is that Padalecki didn't shut the door completely on the series eventually coming to fruition.

"There is a possibility of a Fire Country spin-off. They already did Sheriff Country, and they might be doing another one, Surfside. And I love Max. Max Thieriot is a friend, has been for over 10 years. But right now Anna Fricke and Gen and I, and a few of our gang, are developing a show that we hope will go.

"I mean, TV is so different these days than it used to be, especially because of streaming and whatnot. It used to be appointment television. It was like every Thursday, you watch, and then there will be a week off, but the next Thursday again. Then Supernatural, I think, then moved to Tuesdays, then to Fridays, then to Mondays, then to Wednesdays, so the appointments changed.

"But I’m really hopeful that this project goes. It’s moving forward. We’re still in the process of developing it. It’s just that everything takes a little bit longer these days than it used to. If it was 2000, we’d have an answer by now. But it’s just the way it goes."

For now, it appears that CBS and Padalecki have moved on from Surfside, shifting their focus to the new medical drama. Meanwhile, the next Fire Country spin-off will ironically be a medical drama as well. The untitled spin-off is currently referred to as Edge Med, per Deadline, with Schitt's Creek actor Noah Reid attached to star as Dr. Hudson "Hud" Henshaw. Much like how Morena Baccarin appeared in Fire Country first in Season 2 as Sheriff Mickey Fox before spinning off into her own series, Sheriff Country, Reid will guest star as Dr. Henshaw in Sheriff Country Season 2 before moving on to his own series.

The bad news is that the chances of Fire Country: Surfside getting made seem to be slipping away, as the network, Padalecki, and the Fire Country franchise are all focusing on other projects. A firefighter-themed series set on the beaches of Southern California sounded intriguing. Padalecki's appearances in Fire Country ​​​​​​also hinted at an interesting backstory for his character, as he was the only survivor of a devastating fire, where his entire team, including his younger brother, perished.

’Gossip Girl’s Leighton Meester also stars.

Not to mention, a Fire Country spin-off set in a completely different city solves a big problem between Fire Country and Sheriff Country. We've pointed out before how, despite the fact that the setting for both shows is in the small rural Californian community of Edgewater, it's obvious that they are filmed in different cities. Surfside wouldn't have that problem, since the setting would be completely independent of Edgewater. Not to mention, CBS has seen plenty of success with spin-offs of popular franchises taking place in different cities, including NCIS and CSI.

Padalecki is clearly passionate about his new medical drama project with Fricke, so it's understandable that he wants to focus on that show's development. Nevertheless, we'd still selfishly like him to revisit the Camden Casey role from Fire Country at some point, whether it's in the flagship series or one of its other spin-offs. It looks like the prospective Fire Country: Surfside spin-off is on the back burner for now.

James Bond Franchise Star Officially Reacts to Robert Pattinson Casting

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Lade Omotade is a News and Features Author at Collider, covering everything from casting announcements and streaming trends to behind-the-scenes stories and the cultural impact of today's biggest film and TV franchises. Since joining the team in 2024, she has written more than 1,000 news stories, with her work also cited by outlets like AOL and IMDb.

We finally have our new James Bond, but fans still aren't satisfied. As announced yesterday, Robert Pattinson has officially taken up the mantle, becoming the seventh actor to lead the long-running 007 franchise, following Sean Connery, George Lazenby, Roger Moore, Timothy Dalton, Pierce Brosnan, and Daniel Craig. Pattinson's casting came as quite a surprise after October reports named Callum Turner, Josh O'Connor, and Jack Lowden among the final candidates under consideration.

Amazon MGM Studios is aiming to release the next James Bond film in theaters in 2028, with production expected to begin sometime in 2027. Dune director Denis Villeneuve is at the helm, and Steven Knight (Peaky Blinders) is writing the script. David Heyman and Amy Pascal are among the producers, while casting is being handled by Nina Gold, who previously said that whoever inherits the role would need to commit to potentially three or more films.

Despite the mixed reception to the James Bond news, Villeneuve, who recently worked with Pattinson on Dune: Part Three, welcomed the actor to the beloved franchise in a statement, saying the actor "has the timeless soul of 007 and brings a spirit that is completely new." And as opinions continue to trickle in, Pattinson's former co-star recently weighed in during an interview with Andy Cohen on SiriusXM Front Row.

Ralph Fiennes, who appeared in three previous James Bond films and co-starred with Pattinson in Harry Potter and the Goblet of Fire, sadly isn't ready to let go of Craig's Bond just yet. "I'm sorry. I'm in love with Daniel Craig still," Fiennes admitted. "It's hard. I've got the wrench in my heart." The Conclave star then clarified that he believes Pattinson is up to the task of playing Bond. "No, he's a great actor," he said. "I'm sure he'll be great."

Fiennes joined the James Bond universe in 2012's Skyfall as Gareth Mallory, a high-ranking intelligence official. At the end of the film, Judi Dench's MI6 chief, M, dies, and Mallory takes over as the new M. Fiennes reprised the role in 2015's Spectre and 2021's No Time to Die. He isn't the only former 007 actor to have shared the screen with Pattinson. Brosnan, who played Bond in four films from 1995 to 2002, portrayed the Twilight star's on-screen father in 2010's Remember Me. Pattinson has also worked with Jeffrey Wright, who portrayed Bond ally Felix Leiter in three of Craig's films. He and Wright co-starred in The Batman, where Wright played Detective Jim Gordon opposite Pattinson's Caped Crusader.

IKEA’s 2027 Color of the Year Is Deeper, Sweeter, and a Little More Daring Than Your Average Neutral

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

You’ll want to bring this jammy shade right into your kitchen.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

As a long year of colorful inspiration winds down, so many design brands are coming out of the woodwork with their 2027 trend predictions. IKEA has just joined the cohort with its 2027 Color of the Year: Wild Fig. Yes, the fiber-rich ficus fruit is setting the standard for the Scandi design destination.

IKEA’s interpretation of the jammy color is deep and dark, with red, lilac, and brown undertones. It’s more imaginative than your typical neutral, but still grounded in nature. “Wild fig weaves together the richness of a deep botanical shade with the personality of a color that isn’t afraid to stand out,” the brand wrote in its release. It fits right in with the earthy color trends we’re anticipating next year.

We’ve been keeping track of all the Color of the Year launches, and there’s a clear theme. Every pick so far can be found in your backyard. Valspar opted for the mid-toned blue of a cloudy sky, while Dutch Boy focused on the reddish-brown clay you’d find in the earth. And there’s been no shortage of greens, from Benjamin Moore’s pine-reminiscent pick to Sherwin-Williams’s bright celery shade. The trends reflect all the peace that’s found in a garden escape, and IKEA’s Wild Fig adds some sweetness and play, daring a bit past the typical tree shades to the fruit that blooms from its leaves.

The key to mastering this color, not unlike the fig jam on your charcuterie board, is what you pair it with. IKEA recommends pairing it with its 2026 Color of the Year, Rebel Pink, or an electric blue for contrast that brings out the character of the shade. And no matter your budget, there are so many easy ways to incorporate the look, from a patterned tray or tablecloth to a low-pile, checked rug. If you want to have some fun with your 2027 refresh, we rounded up some easy pieces to get started below.

Louise Claire Johnson and Krista Webster’s ‘Spinsters’ Cover Reveal and Excerpt is a Delicious 1960’s Throwback

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Oh, and did we mention that a Cosmo legend is featured in the new novel?

We may earn commission from links on this page, but we only recommend products we love. Promise.

Louise Claire Johnson and Krista Webster know a thing or two about building PR empires. And so when the two powerhouses decided to partner up together for a new book, it obviously had to take a look at the industry from a completely different side than what readers are used to seeing. And not only are they telling a brand-new tale that brings the 1960s to life in a brand-new and refreshing way, but it’s also bringing some iconic legends to the mix that you’re definitely going to want to see!

Cosmopolitan has an exclusive first look at Spinsters by Louise Claire Johnson and Krista Webster, which is set to be released on June 29, 2027. The novel follows Rose, Lottie, and Ines, a new trio in the PR world in the 1960s that are not afraid to make their mark alongside the men that we all have seen thanks to the likes of Mad Men. But success comes with some surprising challenges and also some big names who also take notice. What happens to these women over the next 20 years is a story that you absolutely don’t want to miss. Here’s some more info from our friends at Kensington:

The book’s cover absolutely brings the perfect retro vibes and feels like an old iconic ad that we definitely would’ve fallen for back in the day. Check out the official cover below:

Luckily for us, we’re turning back the clock a bit early and even getting a glimpse of a Cosmo legend that’s featured in the book. Check out an exclusive excerpt below! Just make sure to preorder Spinsters so you can read what happens next!

I stepped back into the ballroom, pulse racing. The band was still playing, but my world had tilted. Nothing felt the same.

I was threading through the crowd toward the bar when I heard my name.

I turned to find Helen Gurley Brown, radiant in a sequined mask and velvet gown, a champagne coupe in hand. Of course she’d be here. If there was a room with power to be had and people worth dazzling, Helen would be in it.

“You look marvelous,” she said, studying me with a kind of knowing that felt almost maternal. “But something’s off. You’ve got that storm cloud look.”

I let out a laugh that came out thinner than I intended. “Is it that obvious?”

“Only to a woman who’s been exactly where you are,” she said, steering me toward a quieter alcove away from the crush of bodies. “So. Tell me. What’s on your mind?”

There was something in her tone, the mixture of curiosity and command, that cracked me open. Before I could stop my­self, the words tumbled out: Burt had refused me partnership. Years of loyalty, wins, and sacrifice, and still I was expected to be grateful for scraps.

Helen tilted her head, listening without interrupting. “Men hate sharing power. It’s practically coded into their DNA. I can’t say I’m surprised.” She touched my arm. “My offer still stands, you know. Come run features at Cosmopolitan. You’d thrive.”

The thought was more tempting than ever, but something about it still didn’t sit quite right, either. I didn’t want to inherit someone else’s table; I wanted to build my own.

“I appreciate it,” I said softly. “Truly. But I don’t think I want to be an editor.”

“Then don’t,” Helen said easily. “But don’t waste another de­cade making someone else rich while you do the work. If Burt won’t make room, build your own room. PR is your kingdom; you could start your own agency tomorrow if you wanted.”

The idea struck a nerve deep within me. Start my own. It was an audacious, reckless thought. Suddenly, though, even the champagne in my coupe seemed to fizz with anticipation.

Helen raised her glass to me. “To women who write their own stories.”

We clinked, and for the first time that evening, I felt a jolt of something close to joy.

How I’m Approaching AI Search (And How You Should To)

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Identify practical ways to improve location visibility in AI-generated answers and recommendations.

This rundown shows how to test briefs, audits, or rank reporting with a four-layer system built on your own data.

Invalid traffic on AI Max search doubled to 5.28%. Get the channel-by-channel retail benchmarks before Black Friday.

Learn how to prioritize page fixes, check agent access and measure readiness for your 2027 SEO plan.

Ask the SEJ experts you already read what AI, agents, and algorithm changes mean for your traffic. Private, human to human. $97/mo.

Forget citation hacks and obsessing over prompts. AI search is an opportunity to rethink how we approach marketing, audiences and discoverability.

AI is strangely simple. And we’re overcomplicating it.

In focusing on citation tactics and cheap visibility tricks, we’re missing the bigger picture. As an industry, we’ve got away with poor content for years. Updating the same “best spoons” article three times a week because some other idiot is doing exactly the same thing.

There’s nothing inherently wrong with it. It’s just too SEO-y. We’re missing a much bigger opportunity by framing it through a really narrow lens.

I don’t have the energy or the wherewithal to play catch-up every year or two. So I think of AI search as the ultimate synthesis of your marketing efforts.

Your positioning, reputation, expertise, content, and presence across the internet are all potential inputs into how AI systems understand and represent you.

It’s not just about your website anymore.

I’ve spent a lot of time recently working on AI search, information retrieval, audience research, and discoverability strategies. The more I do, the more convinced I become that we need to approach this as marketers, not SEOs.

But don’t build your entire “marketing” strategy around it. I use marketing in the loosest possible sense of the word.

If you’re in dire need of a refresher, here’s a few parts on the fundamentals of modern information retrieval:

There’s one other concept I haven’t written about in detail: query fan-out. Not because it’s not important, more just because a) it’s pretty dull and b) it isn’t a new concept for SEOs. We’ve been using tools like AlsoAsked for years to understand related questions, adjacent information needs and the wider context surrounding a search.

But we also need to distinguish between three things:

They’re related, but they aren’t interchangeable. Being crawled doesn’t guarantee inclusion in training data. Being retrieved doesn’t guarantee a citation. And a citation doesn’t guarantee commercial value.

Understanding these mechanisms is important – mainly just so you can answer the brain-dead questions people ask you about AI. Building your entire marketing strategy around their current implementation is the action of a halfwit.

Models, retrieval systems, and interfaces will change. An owned audience is a considerably more durable investment. So build one.

This is the biggest change I’ve made to my approach. Not just for AI search, but for traditional SEO.

I don’t begin by asking which prompts a brand should appear in. Or how much search volume a keyword or even topic has. Mainly because my trust in search volume and all forms of visibility is at rock bottom.

So I use SparkToro to investigate where audiences spend time, which websites they visit, who influences them, and which platforms they’re disproportionately likely to use compared with the wider population.

For this audience, I know that if I want to influence them, LinkedIn is a non-negotiable (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

I combine that with traditional search research, AI search behaviour and an assessment of the brand’s existing positioning. You don’t need to overcomplicate this – ask a number of different LLMs about insert brand and record the responses.

I want to answer some relatively straightforward questions:

First, our owned platforms. We use what we’ve learned to improve the way we position ourselves, the services we offer, the language we use, and the content we create.

Let’s try to make things under our control as human and well-targeted as possible.

Second, our wider marketing strategy. We identify the publications, communities, creators, platforms, and people capable of influencing that audience, and decide how to reach them.

Audience affinity with specific Subreddits, via SparkToro (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

You need to know this. Not because it might get your brand mentioned as part of the training data. But because this is where you should be marketing.

Made-up prompt data is out. Real, human-led marketing is in. If we want SEO to be taken seriously, this is the proper evolution.

The goal isn’t to manufacture mentions for an AI model. It’s to build a brand people want to discover, engage with, and recommend.

If those activities also increase your representation in AI systems, wonderful. But that’s a delicious byproduct, not the reason for doing them.

While audience research informs the wider strategy, your website needs to be accessible, understandable, and representative of who you are. That is something reasonably competent SEOs can lead.

Can the relevant search and AI crawlers access your content? Can they render it? Are you inadvertently blocking systems you want to reach?

This means reviewing bot management, robots directives, rendering, indexability, internal linking, site architecture, and the usual technical SEO fundamentals.

A traditional SEO-type audit with a few improvements.

One that understands that not all AI bots serve the same purpose. Training crawlers, search crawlers, and user-triggered retrieval systems can have different access requirements and commercial implications.

Crawlers do different things (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

Especially important if you’re working for – or with – a publisher. Someone whose business may depend on you understanding the wider context.

Most large publishers block AI crawlers, sometimes almost indiscriminately, under the banner of copyright protection. There may be very good commercial reasons for doing so. But those decisions fundamentally change your discoverability strategy.

If you’re restricting access to your content, you need to understand what you’re giving up, what you’re protecting, and where the value might exist instead.

Perhaps that’s content licensing, direct audience relationships or proprietary products.

The role of the SEO changes considerably in an environment where you’re not maximising access. You’re helping the business understand the trade-offs between visibility, intellectual property and commercial value.

It’s like a paywall. You’re restricting reach for commercial purposes. It’s the correct move if played properly.

See Cyrus Shepherd’s excellent test to understand how much search traffic was truly influenced by Google’s AI proposition.

This is where I think modern information retrieval methods and SEO intersect perfectly. I’ve written about how to approach this in Entities Are the Foundation of Modern SEO.

This isn’t about adding some structured data and hoping ChatGPT tries to hump your leg. Spoiler: It won’t.

It’s about making sure that your owned presence accurately represents your positioning and that the wider internet provides credible, independent evidence to support it.

So, we know who we are. We’ve represented ourselves properly. Now we have to rely on *shudders* other people to corroborate this message.

This references a podcast I did with Andy Francos and an article I wrote for Lumar two years ago (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

We’re talking actual, real marketing (not SEO). PR, partnerships, content and audience development.

If the people you’re trying to reach spend their time reading particular publications, following particular experts or participating in particular communities, those are the places worth investing in.

Bollocks to a citation. Your audience is there. You know, people you could actually sell something to.

That might mean publishing original research, collaborating with influential people, appearing on podcasts, creating genuinely useful resources or contributing expertise to relevant publications.

Some of those activities will generate links. Others will generate mentions, relationships, conversations and brand demand – a real representation of site quality in Google.

The more consistently you’re associated with the right subjects, people and organizations, the stronger your wider digital presence can become. That is, in essence, how training data and entity relationships work.

If you’re lucky, some of that evidence may eventually influence how AI systems represent you.

But you won’t care, because you’ll have been making sales by marketing to actual customers.

Modern methods of information retrieval across SEO and AI search share many, if not all of the same fundamentals.

Arguably, AI search has a longer tail to it. But it’s not sustainable, nor sensible, to create a page for every possible thought your audience may have. To perpetuate a false Henry Ford quote:

“If I had asked people what they wanted, they would have said faster horses.” H. Ford, Never

So you need to strike a balance. No one cares about visibility without impact. At least not for long.

There is room to try and test things. Good things. Things real people will care about.

People like Jan-Willem Bobbink and Dan Petrovic highlight some really brilliant things you can test that are eminently sensible for SEO and AI search. Surprise, surprise, they work for people too. It’s marketing.

Let me give you an example. Maybe more accurately, let me steal one of Jan’s:

“I spent the last three weeks on SEO / GEO work for B2B clients, and the changes that made the biggest difference were mostly about information that was missing or hard to find. Buyers run into the same problems on these sites, so you can usually get these fixes approved without having to explain SEO to anyone.”

Being explicit, consistent, and accurate is not an AI hack. It’s just pretty sensible.

Adding pricing pages, case studies, and telling people why you are the best in the business are always good ideas. Even if your pricing pages just say something like prices starting at x, that is a better way of finding the right audience.

And if you want to feature in AI responses, for god’s sake don’t hide your case studies. You need to provide a clear reason for your inclusion. Hard if your best work is hidden.

Statements like “Client Y saw a 30% improvement in deliverability metrics” can noticeably improve your citation rate because a model is literally looking for a reason to include you. You need to give them one. Just as you would a person.

Experience. Expertise. Authority. Trust. Not so stupid after all.

Measurement’s been broken for a decade anyway. It’s just nice for the C-suite to have numbers to report on – even if they’re complete hokum.

You try and figure this out (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

AI visibility’s not much better. A hugely flawed metric given the personalization, lack of prompt data, and completely individual prompts. But it’s better than nothing. Indicative at best, but you need to use something.

People still want to know how they’re represented in AI systems. And so they should – it’s a valuable channel. My preference is to establish a representative set of prompts across four areas:

Track these consistently across the systems that matter to your audience.

Look at whether you’re mentioned, how you’re described, which competitors appear, and which sources are being used. Find out when you start to appear for certain terms and see if it matches your positioning.

I always track my mentions with Alertmouse (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

I appear in AI recommendations around news SEO expertise despite having done nothing specifically to optimize for AI search.

What I have done is spend years working in the industry, publishing, building relationships, contributing expertise, and establishing a reasonably clear on-site and off-site identity.

Not a bad list, although I am not sure ChatGPT is quite nailing the profile pictures for us… (Image Credit: Harry Clarkson-Bennett)

I guarantee you, if you ask Barry Adams, he’ll tell you he’s done nothing special for AI either.

I’ve written what I think are high-quality articles on third-party websites and podcasts, featured in roundups, and built my own distribution platform. It just also happens to work for featuring in AI systems.

AI systems don’t necessarily need to send you traffic to influence commercial outcomes. Which is a good thing, because ChatGPT sends less than 1% of Google’s traffic.

Similarweb’s research found that users who received a brand recommendation in ChatGPT were 2.5 times more likely to visit that brand’s website within the following seven days than users who were recommended a competitor.

Much of that activity happened through subsequent branded searches rather than direct AI referrals. One of those measures of site quality that should be monitored.

AI search can and does function as a brand discovery and consideration channel. I think referral traffic is, at best, a secondary measure of success from LLMs. While they’ve engineered the exit out, you cannot deny the reach these systems have.

Unfortunately, our attribution systems can’t cope with “I saw it here once.” We need to focus on incremental change. The outcomes attributable to marketing.

Establish a baseline. Run a campaign. Measure changes in branded demand, direct visits, conversions, leads, and commercial outcomes. Compare with appropriate benchmarks or control groups where possible.

Keep tracking AI visibility, referrals and citations, but use them as supporting indicators.

Our objective is to establish whether said marketing activity is creating additional business value, not whether ChatGPT has linked to your homepage.

For businesses whose models depend on monetizing information and attracting a click, AI search presents a very different challenge.

They are monetizing other people’s work for their own ill-gotten gain. That is an irrefutable commercial dilemma. Hence all the protectionism. So we need to establish what that cost is and try to find alternative methods of monetization.

In The Generative AI Report Sucks – Let’s Make It Brilliant, I outlined how publishers can combine AI visibility data with traffic dependency, commercial value, audience resilience, and server logs.

We can go some way toward solving actual business problems.

Always, always, always a good thing to do. Regardless of your job.

This opportunity is no different, especially for organizations (like publishers) with substantial authority across particular subjects. They have influence within the wider information ecosystem.

That influence can have commercial value.

Publishers Should Think About AI Visibility. Content licensing, data partnerships, research collaborations, and other commercial arrangements offer opportunities to monetize expertise and proprietary information.

Selling links or undisclosed editorial influence is not a sustainable AI strategy. It introduces reputational, editorial, and search-policy risks.

The more sustainable opportunity is to understand the value of your existing content, expertise, audience, and distribution – and build legitimate commercial products around those assets.

Lots of companies are selling or actively considering selling their archived assets or data to AI companies:

For publishers in particular, this is an opportunity to rethink the value they create beyond the click. See the Daily Mail’s approach to GEO monetization here.

If you’re more on the glass half full side, you’ll be able to sniff out commercial opportunities. Ideally ones that don’t ruin the brand or fall foul of Google’s link payment guidelines.

General advice: If something smells a bit off, it probably is.

If you’re a business looking to take this seriously, there’s an organizational question you need to answer.

Because as great as we can be, it shouldn’t just be your SEO.

This touches technical infrastructure, audience research, brand positioning, content, PR, distribution, commercial partnerships, and measurement. That requires someone who understands the entire ecosystem and, crucially, how to influence people.

Nobody is an expert at all of these things. They’re too different. Too complex.

For larger businesses, I’d be thinking about a Head of Marketing or Head of Discoverability with responsibility for coordinating these activities. Someone who at least knows the basics of each and can herd a clowder of cats.

They need the authority to bring different teams together, allocate resources, challenge existing assumptions and measure outcomes against business objectives.

There’s a reason SEOs rarely graduate to marketing lead positions. We don’t understand wider marketing because we’ve rarely had to. SEO was always the best channel… According to wonky data, anyway.

AI search gives us a very good reason to change that.

And for anyone working in SEO, I think there are three particularly interesting directions.

All three have tremendous value. Limiting yourself to SEO, particularly as a collection of search-engine or LLM-specific tactics, is increasingly short-sighted.

Discoverability has been changing for a decade. We need to think much more broadly about how audiences discover and engage with content. The opportunity is to bring those disciplines together.

A little rebrand and refocus on what you want from your career may be the best thing you ever do.

Stop trying to engineer your way into an AI response. Build a brand worth recommending, an audience worth owning, and a business that benefits whether the citation comes or not.

Enjoy yourself. This will be a better era for marketers and marketing. Your competition is lazy and inept. They’re just faster.

SEO Director at The Telegraph with a decade of experience. Unskilled jiu-jitsu guy. Average chess player. Jack of many things.

GTA 6 studio head: "Multiple characters was a leap of faith" and Rockstar knew it "might backfire"

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

GTA 5's iconic trio had something to prove

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

GTA 5's three switchable characters were decided on "right at the start" of development, but Rockstar always knew that the idea "might backfire."

Speaking to Edge Magazine back in 2013, Aaron Garbut - then art director on GTA 5 and now co-studio head supervising GTA 6 - said that Rockstar new from the beginning that "we wanted to do three switchable characters." But even once the studio had established those characters and the tone of their game, Garbut said that "the initial vision" for the game remained "very rough."

Eventually, Rockstar "just built and evolved and let the game dictate itself," but Garbut acknowledged that "[having] multiple characters was a leap of faith. It was an interesting idea, and it felt like we could do interesting things with it, but it also felt like a change to the core of the game that might backfire." Thankfully, the trio of Michael, Trevor, and Franklin "really worked out" - but Garbut admitted he wasn't sure that would be the case until after that game was out.

"It's so hard during development to get perspective," he said. "You get so used to looking for the problems that it becomes almost impossible to step back and see the positives. On this project, despite living in that world for years, playing the missions again and again, it felt fun. It felt great to experience the world, to see it evolve from the basic building blocks to the finished detailed results with all the scenarios and life added."

I can definitely imagine that in the run-up to GTA 5, the trio of protagonists might have amde for a few sleepless nights at Rockstar. Firstly, the studio was bucking its own trend - of a single protagonist surrounded by a large and zany cast of supporting characters. Second, it was stretching an enormous story between those three, asking the audience to go along with a constantly-changing narrative perspective. Third, and potentially most importantly, it was really challenging the hardware at the time - GTA 5 launched on PS3, remember, and the ability to seamlessly swap across a bustling open world was something that had never been tried before.

By contrast, the duo of Jason and Lucia seems like a dramatically easier sell. For one thing, Rockstar already knows it can achieve this particular feat, and doing it with just two characters definitely seems easier than doing it with three. For another, the hardware should be more comfortable with the challenge. And that's before we even touch on the fact that this is no longer a unique narrative concept anymore - GTA 5 might firmly established it, and then Marvel's Spider-Man 2 helped secure it as a reliable and effective tool for both storytelling and bringing an open world to life.

‘It’s that Glaswegian way’: Leon Osman explains why David Moyes was the ultimate fit for Everton

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation plus sign you up to our newsletter.

The former Toffees midfielder reflects on how his old boss understood the fabric of Liverpool - and why his Everton legacy goes far beyond silverware.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Former Everton midfielder Leon Osman has praised David Moyes’ enduring legacy in the blue half of Merseyside, insisting the Scotsman’s Glaswegian grit made him the ideal fit the club

Moyes transformed Everton’s fortunes during an 11-year spell on Merseyside between 2002 and 2013, consistently guiding the Toffees into the top half of the Premier League despite operating on a fraction of the transfer budgets enjoyed by their top-flight rivals.

While Moyes never lifted a major trophy during his tenure - falling just short in the 2009 FA Cup final against Chelsea - Osman believes his former boss’s ability to punch above his weight cements his status as one of the club’s most vital modern figures.

"For the majority of David Moyes' time in charge of Everton, he's probably overachieved in terms of what he's had to spend in the transfer market, so while he might not have had the success Harry Catterick did in terms of silverware, he's been so important for the club," Osman told Betway.

"He's a great fit for Everton, and I think it's that Glaswegian way that adapts particularly well to Liverpool because there's a real understanding of what the people of the city are like and what they want to see from their football team. Everton is the people's club first and foremost, and the fans want to see the team work hard and show that desire to be successful and that's something David Moyes has always instilled in his teams."

Osman was a central pillar of that Moyes era, making over 400 appearances for his boyhood club after breaking into the senior squad under the manager’s guidance. However, the former England international admitted he initially faced an uphill battle to prove he possessed the physicality required for top-flight football before becoming one of Moyes' most trusted lieutenants.

"He's the man who gave me my shot in the Premier League, so I'll always be thankful to him for that," Osman added. "It did take me a while, though, to convince him I was able and I was ready to play at that level, but once he gave me that chance I think he realised that we had very similar outlooks in terms of style of play and how the game should be played and we had a really great relationship throughout my career."

ULA traces Vulcan Centaur rocket failure to 'inadequate fuel supply' to 1st-stage engines

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

The root cause of the anomaly is still under investigation.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

United Launch Alliance (ULA) is getting a handle on why its Vulcan Centaur rocket failed over the weekend.

That failure, which occurred during a launch from Florida's Space Coast on Sunday (Nov. 15), led to the loss of 40 Amazon Leo internet satellites. ULA soon traced the anomaly to Vulcan Centaur's first stage, and it has now pinpointed the problem area even further.

"While work remains to determine cause, preliminary indications are that the low booster thrust was the result of inadequate fuel supply from the vehicle to the BE-4 engines, not from the performance of the engines," ULA wrote in an update today (Nov. 18).

This is good news for Blue Origin, Jeff Bezos' aerospace company, which builds the BE-4. Two of these engines power Vulcan's first-stage booster, and they're also key to Blue Origin's New Glenn rocket: That heavy lifter features seven or nine BE-4s in its first stage, depending on the configuration. (New Glenn has its own issues at the moment; it exploded during a test on May 28, and Blue Origin is working to return the powerful rocket to flight.)

ULA had already said that Vulcan's six solid rocket boosters (SRBs) and its Centaur V upper stage — which employs a different engine, the RL-10 — seem to perform well on Sunday. Further analysis is firming up that conclusion, according to today's update.

Vulcan has had issues with its SRBs in the past. On the rocket's second and fourth flights — which launched in October 2024 and February of this year, respectively — an SRB nozzle burned through and fell off about 30 seconds into flight. On both occasions, Vulcan overcame the problem and successfully delivered its payload to orbit.

In the wake of the February flight, ULA and Northrop Grumman, which builds the SRBs, tweaked the nozzle design — a fix that apparently worked, based on Sunday's nominal performance.

Sunday's launch was the fifth ever for the two-stage Vulcan Centaur, the successor to ULA's workhorse Atlas V rocket. Vulcan debuted in January 2024 and has now experienced issues on three of its five flights, though Sunday's was the only one that ended in failure.

Vulcan was out of action for about nine months after the February SRB issue. It's unclear how long its current grounding will last; that depends on what ULA's anomaly investigation, which the U.S. Federal Aviation Administration is overseeing, ends up finding.

Google Play wants Android apps to nail foldable basics by April 2027

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

Google Play is introducing new adaptive app and game quality requirements next year to elevate the Android foldable experience.

Starting April 2027, the Play Store will require developers to meet Tier 3 of the Android adaptive app quality guidelines for foldables.

Referred to as “Adaptive ready” or “basic” support, Tier 3 requires apps not to use letterboxing (or other compatibility modes) and to fill the entire screen. During rotation, resizing, and folding/unfolding, apps must preserve scroll position, text input, and media playback. Apps must also support multi-window environments.

Apps that do not meet this quality bar will no longer be able to publish or update on Google Play, and may have reduced visibility on the store.

At the same time, Google Play will require foldable device screenshots for app listings: “Providing foldable screenshots by April 2027 will become a standard submission requirement when publishing new apps or updating your store listing.”

By August 2027, new and updated apps will be required to target Android 17 (API level 37).

Over time, this “quality framework will progressively expand to incorporate Tier 2 and Tier 1 standards across foldables, tablets, Googlebooks, and Android XR.”

Apps that meet Tier 2 (better) or Tier 1 (best) will get “premier store badging, editorial spotlighting, and enhanced merchandising across Google Play as the platform bar elevates.”

On the gaming front, titles will need to add foldable screenshots to listings by April 2027, while Tier 3 large screen quality guidelines go into effect in August 2027.

Safe Places to Earn Interest – Match One to When You’ll Need the Money

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Money you can’t afford to lose can still earn interest in high-yield savings, money market funds, CDs, Treasury bills, or I bonds. Pick the one you can cash out without a penalty by the month you’ll need the money, and skip any product sold as safe that pays far more than Treasury bills.

No investment pays a high return without the risk of a loss, and money set aside for a down payment, a tuition bill, or an emergency can’t take that risk.

Each option below is protected by FDIC insurance (NCUA insurance at a credit union), the U.S. government, or a fund’s short-term holdings. What separates them is how soon you can get your money back and what it costs to get it early, so the date you’ll need the money decides which one fits.

Here’s what protects each one, how fast you can reach the money, and the catch.

Use Treasury bills as the yardstick for any rate you’re offered. The Federal Reserve’s latest daily figures put the three-month Treasury yield, the annual rate the government pays to borrow for three months, at 4.32%. A product sold as low-risk that pays 7% or 8% does so because it carries a risk you can’t see. The SEC flags “high returns with little or no risk” as a sign of a Ponzi scheme. The one safe exception is the I bond, whose rate follows inflation and can top Treasury yields when inflation runs high.

Money you might need any day, like an emergency fund, belongs in high-yield savings or a money market fund, not a savings account earning the national average, which the FDIC puts at 0.39%. For an expense due within a year, buy a Treasury bill or a CD that matures before it. For a goal one to five years away, line up CDs to mature by the date, or roll Treasury bills over until then. Put only money you can leave alone for at least a year into I bonds, and use a short-term bond fund only for money that could ride out a small loss.

Before you buy a bond fund, look up its duration on the fund’s fact sheet. Duration measures how much a bond’s price is likely to move when rates change. By FINRA’s rule of thumb, a fund with a two-year duration loses about 2% for each percentage point that rates rise.

Say you’re saving for a down payment in two years. A CD ladder, a set of CDs that come due one after another before your closing date, has every dollar ready when you buy. So do Treasury bills you roll into new ones as each matures. Dividend stocks don’t, since a steady dividend won’t stop the share price from falling the month you need to sell. That’s the reason to keep money you’ll need within five years out of stocks.

Write down the month you’ll need the money, cross off every option you can’t cash out by then without a penalty, and put the money in whichever of the rest pays the most today.

The 101 money moves that matter most, from the foundations to protecting what you’ve built.

We do not include every company or offer available in the marketplace.
