Your Business Is Saving Hours With AI. So Why Are Customers Still Waiting?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

Before investing in another automation tool, ask whether the time you’re saving is actually improving your customers’ experience.

Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.

Recently, while working on a healthcare client’s project, my team encountered a problem that had nothing to do with our ability to do the work.

We needed access to the client’s digital platforms. What should have been a straightforward process became a series of emails, permission issues, authentication problems and confusion over which accounts controlled what.

The actual work wasn’t the obstacle. Getting everything in place so we could do the work was.

I’ve encountered different versions of this problem throughout my consulting career. Teams have the expertise. The technology exists. Everyone wants to move forward. Yet progress stalls because the underlying process is more complicated than anyone realized.

It made me think about how businesses are approaching artificial intelligence. We’re investing in tools that promise to make work faster, but how often do we stop to examine what’s slowing the business down in the first place?

When entrepreneurs think about productivity, they naturally focus on the time employees spend completing tasks. How quickly can we produce a report? How much time can we save creating content? Can AI respond to routine inquiries? Those are reasonable questions.

But consider what happened in the healthcare project. Automating another task wouldn’t have resolved who owned the account, who could authorize access or how the team should handle authentication. Those were process and accountability problems.

This is why I believe every business should examine its workflows before investing heavily in automation.

I’ve previously written about why entrepreneurs should examine workflows before hiring more employees. The same principle applies to technology.

If responsibilities are unclear and information is scattered, automation may simply help a flawed process move faster.

A business owner might celebrate saving 20 hours a week. The customer is asking a different question: Why am I still waiting?

That gap is easy to overlook because internal performance metrics don’t always reflect the customer experience.

Imagine improving the speed of customer service responses while leaving the underlying resolution process unchanged. Customers might receive answers sooner but still wait days for their problems to be addressed.

The business reports an efficiency improvement. Customers experience little difference. I see a similar disconnect in marketing. Companies sometimes invest in generating more leads without examining what happens after those leads arrive.

As I explored in my article about why businesses can spend more on marketing while attracting fewer customers, more activity doesn’t automatically translate into better commercial results. The lesson applies to AI as well.

Measure the outcome, not just the activity that produced it.

One thing I’ve learned from managing complex projects is that technology cannot replace clear ownership. Someone still needs to make decisions, resolve exceptions and take responsibility when something goes wrong.

Before automating a process, I ask three questions:

If those answers aren’t clear, technology isn’t the first thing I would fix.

For example, automated appointment reminders can reduce administrative effort. But if patients cannot easily reschedule or reach someone when they need help, the experience remains frustrating.

The same is true for sales, onboarding and service delivery. Customers appreciate convenience, but they also want confidence that someone is paying attention.

This may be the most important question entrepreneurs should ask about AI.

Saving time is only valuable when businesses put that time to better use. This is the AI productivity trap: Faster work doesn’t necessarily mean faster growth. The real opportunity lies in using that efficiency to improve customer relationships, solve problems and create measurable business value.

Suppose a new system saves your team 10 hours every week. What do you do with that time? Do you reinvest it in customer relationships? Improve service quality? Reduce expenses? Give employees more opportunities to solve problems that technology cannot handle?

Or does that time simply disappear into additional meetings and administrative work? The answer matters.

In my earlier Entrepreneur article about why business growth doesn’t always improve profitability, I discussed the importance of looking beyond top-line results. Productivity deserves the same scrutiny.

An efficient business isn’t necessarily a profitable one, and a productive team isn’t automatically delivering a better customer experience.

Entrepreneurs need to decide how efficiency gains will create value before assuming they will.

My advice is simple: Choose one process that repeatedly creates problems. It might be customer onboarding, appointment scheduling, account access, proposal approvals or resolving support requests. Follow that process from beginning to end.

Talk to the people involved. Identify where information gets lost, approvals stall or responsibilities become unclear. Then ask whether the problem requires automation at all.

Sometimes the answer is a better system. Sometimes it’s eliminating an unnecessary approval. Sometimes it’s giving one person clear responsibility for following through.

Make one change, measure what happens, and expand what works. You may discover that the most valuable improvement costs considerably less than another software subscription.

I’m enthusiastic about what AI can do for entrepreneurs, especially smaller businesses competing with organizations that have significantly greater resources. But I don’t believe adopting more technology automatically makes a company more competitive.

The healthcare project reinforced something I’ve seen repeatedly: Even capable teams with good technology can struggle when the underlying process isn’t working.

Your customers don’t see how many hours you save internally. They notice whether you deliver what you promised, respond when they need you and make their lives easier.

Before celebrating how much faster your business has become, make sure your customers can feel the difference.

Recently, while working on a healthcare client’s project, my team encountered a problem that had nothing to do with our ability to do the work.

We needed access to the client’s digital platforms. What should have been a straightforward process became a series of emails, permission issues, authentication problems and confusion over which accounts controlled what.

The actual work wasn’t the obstacle. Getting everything in place so we could do the work was.

Round Up: GTA 6 Is Not Just the Most Acclaimed Game of 2026, It's the Highest-Rated Game of All Time

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The initial reviews have arrived for GTA 6, and it's safe to say the game has been an absolute smash hit with critics.

The metascore has settled on a 99, making it one point off a "perfect" score of 100. In a year stuffed with fantastic games big and small, it's become the highest rated title of 2026.

More than that, though, it's the highest rated game of all time.

Review GTA 6 (PS5) - The Greatest Open World Game Ever Made

Reaching 99 is almost unheard of; only one other game has achieved the same level of acclaim — The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time (1998).

However, GTA 6 has held onto its 99 score after more than 160 reviews; Ocarina of Time's metascore counts just 22 — though that's of course indicative of the era in which it released. There are simply more gaming outlets now, but that just means it's tougher to get consistent scores.

Rockstar's crime caper has received insanely consistent acclaim, and that might just give it an edge over the Nintendo classic. It's incredibly rare to see near-total agreement on a subjective work like this.

Critics are unanimous in their praise of GTA 6's open world, the relationship between protagonists Jason and Lucia, and the complex systems behind its myriad characters, activities, and missions.

Here are some snippets from just a few of the reviews — including our own, of course.

"GTA 6 is the deepest, densest, most accomplished open world since Red Dead Redemption 2. Rockstar has set a new sandbox standard here, with a world that feels alive and populated with real people; Leonida is a place where it appears almost anything can happen at any time."

"Somehow, GTA 6 lives up to the hype. Rockstar Games has crafted the most incredible open world I've ever experienced in a video game, packed with so much detail that I'll want to continue exploring it for months and likely years to come. It's coupled with a campaign that's full of extremely entertaining missions, led by two main characters who I've become much more invested in than I'd anticipated."

"Like all videogame protagonists, Jason and Lucia are ciphers through which the player exerts their will on the digital world. And yet, through the co-authored interplay of the characters Rockstar has written, and the people you personally tune them into becoming, they form one of the great partnerships in entertainment: beautiful and deadly, open to the possibilities of their world and, despite the violence they inflict on those beyond their circle, driven forward not by fear, but by love."

"A game of opposites and awe, minute detail and impossible scale, Rockstar pushes back the horizon of video games once again with GTA 6. An irreducible mix of silliness, intimacy, grand ideas and rock 'n' roll action - and a load more after that."

"Rockstar Games' latest release is a monumental achievement in storytelling, immersion, gameplay, and breadth of content."

"GTA 6 is the highest-fidelity sandbox of its generation. Rockstar has built an open world of truly astonishing scale, scope, and quality. There's a degree of sophistication to everything GTA 6 does, and there's no aspect of the series' gameplay that hasn't been improved over past instalments."

"Only Rockstar commands the time and money necessary to make a game as dedicated to small details. And only Rockstar can execute on the realising such a granular vision unimpeachably. The obsession with the minutia both explains and justifies the 13-year wait. This game is the culmination of everything the developer has previously achieved in the open-world space that pushes the genre to heights that, in all likelihood, no other developer will be able to reach. GTA 6 is an astonishing open-world action game that I’d wager won’t be outdone until Grand Theft Auto 7."

"Exquisitely detailed, endlessly ripe for experimentation, and uncompromisingly engineered, Grand Theft Auto 6 is the single most impressive video game I have ever played."

"Grand Theft Auto 6 is a modern masterpiece. Rockstar's developers have created a remarkable open world that excels in storytelling, gameplay, visuals, music and technology to achieve something truly unmatched. Minor performance issues don’t dull the sparkle of an absolute must-play experience. GTA 6 was more than worth the wait."

‘Elsinore’ Review: Andrew Scott’s Towering Performance as a Dying Actor Can’t Overcome AIDS-Movie Awards Bait Clichés

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

[Editor’s note: This review was originally published on September 5, 2026 during the 2026 Telluride Film Festival. Focus Features will release the film in theaters Friday, November 20.]

There’s no question Andrew Scott, the 49-year-old Irish actor, has earned his position as one of the finest contemporary actors going. He’s in demand, but choosing wisely, and in roles that slyly play off his own status as an out gay man (a rare thing indeed) but with something off-center beneath, often a secret. He played Tom Ripley, one even more sociopathic than Matt Damon’s own iconically calculating Adonis, to devastating effect in the sinister Netflix series “Ripley,” there more explicitly grappling with the Patricia Highsmith character’s ambiguous (but was it?) sexuality. He played the Hot Priest who turned down a chance at heterosexual, secular love in “Fleabag,” of course, and starred in “All of Us Strangers” as a lonely adult orphan living in a glittering tower, bracing himself for romantic intimacy with Paul Mescal. Related Stories Jim Cummings Wakes Up to a Post-One-Night-Stand Crisis in ‘DISC’ — Watch the Short ‘Artificial’ Trailer: Andrew Garfield Stuns in Luca Guadagnino’s Sharp, Shocking, Funny Portrayal of OpenAI CEO Sam Altman

I also saw him playing up to nine roles in Anton Chekhov’s “Vanya” onstage in New York City last year as men and women of all sorts, showing an actor who can shimmy between genders and across ranges. He has the unmistakable warmth of someone you should trust, but with intrigue behind the curtain of many of his performances to suggest that maybe you shouldn’t. He’s that good.

Scott has now taken on what will, to this point, I suppose, go down as “the role of a lifetime” in director Simon Stone’s rueful, pained, and unremarkable new film drama “Elsinore” as Scottish actor Ian Charleson, who died of HIV/AIDS complications while portraying Hamlet at the National Theatre in London in 1990. Charleson’s story is unlikely to be one most are familiar with. Even the most public of deaths among the most publicly gay of people often still remain in hiding, though Charleson wasn’t out himself. He resisted pressures from his inner circle to announce his disease amid a stage run that miraculously lasted 24 performances. Actors then were afraid of damaging their reputations. It’s not that different now, even as many advancements have made HIV/AIDS feel locally like a plague of the past.

A blond-headed Scott throws himself valiantly into the role of Charleson with the urgency and commitment of any of his best work, but the problem isn’t his: It’s that the movie surrounding him dulls the greatness of his performance with its conventional storytelling, and over-complication in post. “Elsinore” is one of those movies where you can feel the filmmaker — here, Stone, known for both stage and screen — actively laboring to “open up the play” with the opportunities of film. But “Elsinore” was never a play. Instead, it’s a movie where every emotional beat worth lingering on, especially Charleson’s swan-song performances as Hamlet, is churned into a frenzied montage of editing that won’t let the material take a breath. Or the audience sink in.

Charleson played an Olympian athlete in the Best Picture-winning 1981 film “Chariots of Fire,” and an Anglican priest in the next year’s Best Picture topper, “Gandhi.” That was after playing an outré bisexual in the 1978 film “Jubilee,” directed by his former lover, the very openly gay director Derek Jarman, who died of AIDS in 1994. Aside from a handful of film credits, including one alluded to in “Elsinore” as Dario Argento’s giallo “Opera,” Charleson otherwise kept to the stage. Scott’s Charleson at one point in this film opines about how much more satisfying stage work is for an actor without an editor there to chop you up to pieces.

Stone, working from a screenplay by Stephen Beresford (himself a playwright), does not follow his protagonist’s lead. Every thoughtful consideration of Kaposi’s sarcoma lesions, or the retching of blood into buckets under the care of dutiful practitioner Dr. Margaret Johnson (Olivia Colman) to show the terrible realities of the disease, is whipped into what feels like a feature-length trailer. The best scenes out of the “Hamlet” production detailed here are tossed into a rushed montage near the movie’s end that gives us no sense of what we are promised is a performance of enormity. (The play in 1990 was directed by Richard Eyre, later the filmmaker behind features like the campy diva battle “Notes on a Scandal.” He’s played in “Elsinore” by Johnny Flynn.)

A couple of things are going on: “Elsinore” triggers in a watchful gay viewer’s senses the signifiers of what would be the AIDS awards-bait movie of serious gravitas. Tom Hanks’ Oscar-winning performance in “Philadelphia.” Ed Harris in “The Hours.” Everyone who came out of the cringey “Dallas Buyers Club” with Academy Awards in 2014. There are others, though more lost to time. We become suspicious of this device, especially when paired with great acting. Is HIV/AIDS being exploited in the package of a would-be Oscar hit? Or are we showing young queer audiences how it was, how we got to where we are, and how where that is still isn’t perfect? It can be both, but the structure of “Elsinore” tips toward the former.

Then, there’s a veritable, cheeky who’s-who of British gay literati and luminaries lingering about “Elsinore’s” 1989 London milieu that’s irresistible in theory, but often drab in execution. There’s Eyre, of course, and Jarman (David Dawson, as excellent and tender and wounded here as he was in his screen breakthrough “My Policeman”). The afterburn of his relationship with Charleson lingers affectingly, though. After some drinks, he invites Charleson home, but they instead part with one of those tacit kisses shared by people who once loved each other but can’t make it work anymore. There’s too much history here. The lingering scorches of Charleson’s secret affairs and friendships with various (and out) men make for the best parts of “Elsinore,” but there’s no life to the telling. (Joe Locke plays a young man also ailing in Johnson’s AIDS clinic alongside Charleson.)

The pallid grayness of Mike Eley’s cinematography is probably supposed to mimic the finality of HIV/AIDS taking hold, but there’s no passion aesthetically to counterbalance what becomes absolute misery onscreen to watch. I’m not in the business of comparing one gay social drama to another, but Robin Campillo’s 2017 Cannes-winning French drama “Beats Per Minute” imbued an albeit more activist-oriented story of ACT UP fighting against AIDS suppression in Paris with such heat and spectacle. Watching someone die of AIDS in a room becomes a bore unless anyone is given a reason to keep on living.

That reason is, of course, supposed to be Charleson’s performance in “Hamlet,” which is relegated to a whirlwind sequence at the movie’s end after many pained rehearsals where he refused to drop out (and where Johnson encouraged him to). Scott has a mesmerizing hold on his scenes, and he’s compelling to watch in his repartee with Colman as Johnson, who treated the disease at a time when it was most stigmatized. She named her groundbreaking HIV testing clinic after him; that’s how much he impacted her. We are told how much Charleson mattered to people, but never shown it. Which unfortunately shuffles off “Elsinore,” named after the Danish town where “Hamlet” was set and where its main character died, off to the realm of HIV/AIDS movies seeking prestige over deep feeling.

“Elsinore” premiered at the 2026 Telluride Film Festival. Focus Features releases it starting November 20.

Report: European heavyweights enter transfer race for €100m Liverpool target

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Sports  |  Read Source →

Liverpool are one of the most well-known football teams in the world.

The Anfield outfit have always been challenging for titles and silverware with the expectations of their fans rather high.

That is, especially after what Jurgen Klopp did while in charge of the club.

Since his departure, the Reds have won a Premier League title and have spent well over £600 million on new players.

And, while this may seem like a lot of money, many more changes need to take place before the Reds are back to their best.

So, not only does this mean more money needs to be spent but, it also means that Liverpool will have to cast their net far and wide to sign all the players that they need to.

Bayern Munich and Barcelona have joined the race for Aleksey Batrakov

One of the main problems Liverpool have faced this season is a lack of creativity.

The Anfield outfit have tried to rely on Florian Wirtz to create their chances but, he's constantly failed to meet expectations.

This has forced other players among the Reds' ranks to try and step up but, with the club currently sitting fourth in the table, that clearly hasn't worked out.

So, that's why a lot of Liverpool's recent transfer links have been with playmakers such as Aleksey Batrakov.

The Russian has been in fine form for Galatasaray this term with four of his seven goals coming in the Champions League.

In fact, his form has been so good that, according to Turkish outlet Takvim, Bayern Munich and Barcelona have also entered the race for the 21-year-old.

"Galatasaray's 21-year-old Russian star, Aleksey Batrakov, has attracted the attention of leading European clubs," they wrote.

"While Barcelona and Bayern Munich are reportedly watching the young footballer closely, Galatasaray's management has taken steps to increase his €55 million release clause to €100 million."

Liverpool can't spend €100m on Aleksey Batrakov

Right now, Batrakov has a release clause worth around €55 million.

In pounds, that equates to around £45 million.

Obviously, this is a lot of money for a 21-year-old but, considering Liverpool spent £40 million on Milos Kerkez, it doesn't seem all that bad.

Since joining Galatasaray in the summer, Batrakov has contributed to 10 goals in 13 games.

The Turkish Super Lig outfit have mostly played him as a number 10 but, there has been the odd occasion when he was used as a number 8.

Compared to someone such as Wirtz or Dominik Szoboszlai, he may not seem as good but, from the games he's featured in this season, you can tell that the youngster has a bright future ahead of him.

Right now, it's unclear what Liverpool are going to do but, if Galatasaray do manage to up his release clause to €100 million that would be far too much money to risk on a relatively unproven youngster.

Pellegatti: Calvelli, Amorim, Krösche – why it may have been ‘better to lose against Cagliari’

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Carlo Pellegatti has painted a positive picture regarding AC Milan’s developments, one that puts last season’s collapse into perspective.

Rewinding to the end of May, it felt like Milan’s entire world have caved in. A 2-1 defeat at home to Cagliari – a game that they led in early on – in the final round of the season condemned the Rossoneri to a fifth-placed finish, meaning they missed out on Champions League football again.

Read SempreMilan ad-free and get access to exclusive news. Click here for a free trial!

This is after Massimiliano Allegri had the team in a title fight going into the Spring, but ultimately the decline was near-vertical towards the end of the campaign. He paid the price for this, as did the CEO Giorgio Furlani, sporting director Igli Tare and chief scout Geoffrey Moncada.

In his latest column for MilanNews, Carlo Pellegatti has put forward the notion that the defeat to the Sardinians might turn out to be a blessing. Why? Because the reset that followed on a sporting and managerial level seems to already be taking a positive shape.

“Krosche and delight! It’s a somewhat obvious joke, but it captures the expectations of AC Milan fans. When will he arrive? November 27th, as I’ve been told, or in the first ten days of December, or even – I really hope not – January 1st?” he began.

“The difference in dates for the announcement of his new role is by no means insignificant, because the January transfer window becomes a crucial turning point for Milan’s fortunes, both today and in the future.

“The sooner the Head of Football sits down at Casa Milan, the better he can weave his web to strengthen the team in the winter transfer window. This should have been the job of a year ago, but was not accomplished by a shortsighted management team that never understood the difference between costs and investments.

“It’s also a shame that Igli Tare is a person who respects roles and is attentive to internal balances. He had to, and this is his great regret, bypass those who managed Milan, to speak with Gerry Cardinale and explain the importance of the arrival of a striker and a defender, who would have allowed them to be in the Champions League today.

“I’m using a very bold paradox, one I’ve never admitted. If failing to qualify for the Champions League was the reason that pushed Gerry Cardinale to fire the key figures of the three worst and most disastrous years in Milan’s history, then, given the exciting future prospects, it would have been better to lose against Cagliari, which avoided prolonging the agony of our dear old AC Milan. A painful sacrifice that, I hope, will be repaid with success in the years to come.

“With a Cardinale who was too absent and distant, guilty, by his own admission, of having delegated the management of his club to unsuitable people, but who now becomes the protagonist of this new way of operating, reminiscent of the good old days.

“I obviously feel sorry for Tare and Allegri, who, despite having their responsibilities, were victims of a confusing, chaotic, and divisive situation, and through no fault of their own.

“Returning to Krosche, I hope he quickly finds his feet in this Milan adventure that awaits him. I continue to believe that a highly experienced and highly skilled Italian operative, to work alongside the German director, could and can be of help to him and Milan, given the obvious difficulties of integrating into a world that is completely different from the German one.

“Gerry Cardinale and his chief assistant, Massimo Calvelli, are convinced of the wisdom of their choice, after meeting several times with Markus Krosche. I think it’s crucial that he’s not only a skilled market operator, but also a valid on-field contact for Ruben Amorim.

“The Portuguese will then be able to discuss potential choices and decisions with his Head of Football. In short, it was a position in the organisational chart that was culpably neglected, with the results that were seen, until Gerry Cardinale himself imposed Igli Tare, who, in any case, did not have an easy job.

“I mentioned Massimo Calvelli, who, in the media, always appears to be somewhat out of the spotlight. Certainly not in operational terms, because, from what I know, he was the absolute protagonist and architect of transactions that brought approximately €825m into Milan’s coffers.

“He was certainly a skilled architect of the return of Adidas as a shirt sponsor, but also a key figure in the acquisition of Generali, which will give its name to the new stadium, this time with the collaboration, it is said, of Paolo Scaroni.

“Therefore, he is a valuable executive who works behind the scenes, but with excellent results in terms of economic and financial aspects and also for the club’s image.”

Brighton midfielder Yasin Ayari has hinted he could leave the club next summer, with Chelsea and Manchester United monitoring his situation ahead of a potential transfer.

The Sweden international has enjoyed an impressive campaign under Fabian Hurzeler, attracting attention from several European clubs as uncertainty continues to surround his long-term future at the Amex Stadium.

According to a TEAMtalk report, Ayari has refused to commit to anything beyond the current season, and his contract situation is becoming increasingly complicated.

The 23-year-old has scored three goals in nine Premier League appearances this season, including the winning goal against Manchester City. His performances have helped Brighton establish themselves among the division’s leading sides.

The midfielder’s uncertain contractual situation could present an attractive opportunity for interested clubs, with Chelsea and Manchester United among the sides linked with his signature.

AC Milan, AS Roma, Galatasaray and Fenerbahce are also understood to be monitoring developments, potentially creating significant competition for his services.

Brighton are keen to retain an important member of Hurzeler’s squad, but negotiations over new terms have hit snags. The Seagulls also possess an option to extend his existing agreement until 2028.

However, the growing interest in his services could complicate their efforts to secure his future.

Ayari’s technical ability, energy and versatility make him an attractive prospect for Chelsea and United, both of whom could benefit from additional quality in midfield.

With his contract situation approaching a crucial stage, Brighton will be desperate to avoid losing one of their most promising performers without securing an appropriate financial return.

A U.S. Film Incentive Would Help These Three States Most of All

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

New York, New Jersey and Georgia are poised to reap the greatest benefit thanks to unlimited state tax credits.

Hollywood studios are not banking on a U.S. tax incentive just yet. Though President Trump reaffirmed his support for the measure in a Truth Social post on Nov. 13, insiders say there is only a “narrow path” to approving the bill by the end of the year.

Still, supporters are allowing themselves to dream about the potential effect if it does pass, using words like “massive” and “game changer.” Related Stories Alycia Baumgardner vs. Elif Nur Turhan on ESPN Unlimited: Here’s Where to Watch the Title Bout Live Online Nike Drops New Air Jordan 11 Retro 'Green Screen' Sneakers: Here’s Where To Buy a Pair Online

“It’s gonna be a golden age of productions,” says Brian Papworth, executive director of the Coalition for American Production, who has been lobbying for the measure at the Capitol.

Three states — New York, New Jersey and Georgia — offer generous film credits and stand to gain the most, according to several production executives who spoke to Variety. Producers love shooting in those states not because of the credit amount — but because credits are automatic.

California has more production capacity — i.e., crews and stages — than anywhere else in the world and will benefit too, especially as work spills over from an overbooked New York. But it is not producers’ first choice, because it sticks to its $750 million incentive cap.

Georgia has no limit on film rebates, and in New Jersey and New York, the caps are not enforced. New York’s program is budgeted at $800 million a year. But since making the formula more generous last year, the state has blown through that limit — handing out $1.7 billion in credits in the 12 months ending July 1.

New Jersey’s program is also growing fast, and has $3 billion in reserves once the state completes deals with studio partners. Both states pull from future-year allocations when demand exceeds the cap, so producers never have to worry about being turned away. All three states also allow producers to claim “above the line” expenses, such as big-ticket salaries for directors, producers and actors. For the studios, that puts those states in a class by themselves.

Adding a 20% to 30% federal incentive would create an effective subsidy of 50% or more (in New York, more like 60%). That far exceeds the incentives offered in the U.K., Canada and Australia. If the U.S. incentive were to take effect on Jan. 1, 2027, studios would likely start bringing feature films and other productions back by the end of the year.

Filming in New York and New Jersey is already hot, and it’s starting to heat up in Georgia after a few down years. Adding a federal incentive would pour nitroglycerine on the fire — at least in those states. California, meanwhile, belongs to a second tier of states — also including Pennsylvania, Massachusetts, New Mexico and Kentucky — that would not gain as much because their programs are capped.

“A federal credit lifts all boats,” says Ed Lammi, a retired production exec who worked at Sony Pictures TV. “It would lift our boat too. I still think we need to basically eliminate the cap in California.”

California’s program has three major problems in the eyes of studios and producers. First, the state does not borrow from future-year allocations. Second, because of that, productions must compete for a finite amount of credits, which requires a cumbersome application process. And third, the state does not include above-the-line expenses.

Michael Kagan, co-head of unscripted at Range Media Partners, has grown increasingly frustrated as his reality competition show clients have been forced to leave California to work.

“The state hasn’t done a good job of helping preserve these projects,” he says, noting that “American Idol” left for Atlanta and “America’s Got Talent” and “The Voice” are also eyeing a move. “Now there’s three large-scale network competition shows in L.A. There used to be 15 or 20.”

Those shows became eligible for California tax credits last year, but so far only one — Mark Rober’s “Schooled!” — has received them.

Competition shows used to shoot in L.A. by default, Kagan says. “Now when they’re starting, they look at the U.K., Canada, Ireland and Timbuktu.”

A federal incentive would help bring those projects back from overseas — but maybe not to L.A.

“I think it’ll be great in general,” Kagan says. “But I don’t think it’ll be great for the state of California.”

GTA 6 launch day: Live updates on the biggest game of the year

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Other  |  Read Source →

We're playing, you're playing, everyone's playing. Here's a live report on everything we find out about Rockstar's biggest game.

Grand Theft Auto 6 is finally almost here. The biggest game of the year, if not the decade, is now available on the PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X/S, and reviews went up today. The game officially goes live at midnight local time, so depending on your time zone, you may already be deep into Rockstar's new Vice City adventure.

We'll be playing and pulling together all the biggest discoveries and reactions from gamers (and our editors), and we hope you took our advice and downloaded the 212GB file in advance — and the firmware update you'll need to play on PS5.

If you're still downloading, make sure you're set up for success with the best graphics settings, too, even if you won't see anything beyond 30 fps. PS5 Pro owners will get ray-tracing at least. And PC gamers? You'll have to wait until the game lands there in 2027.

If you've somehow avoided the reviews and coverage so far, expect another sandbox adventure with new game mechanics, the ability to swap between the two protagonists and new criminal profiles that appear to pull from Red Dead Redemption 2. Because this is GTA and you will be breaking the law.

I'm excited to see how Rockstar upgrades gunplay and weapon mechanics, as a lot has changed since GTA 5 landed 13 years ago. My colleague Devindra Hardawar will be playing, while our coworker Kris Holt has already spent hours on it. Google is forcing Android app developers to properly support foldables

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: World  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Now that the iPhone Duo is foldable is here, Google wants to make sure apps on Android foldables keep up.

Now that the iPhone Duo is foldable is here, Google wants to make sure apps on Android foldables keep up.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Now that Apple’s foldable is here, Google wants to make sure apps on Android foldables are a good experience. Starting in April 2027, Google is going to require Android developers to make sure their apps have a baseline level of support for foldables. If they don’t, developers risk not being able to publish or update their apps or have reduced visibility for their apps on Google Play.

Google says Android apps must meet its “Tier 3 — Adaptive ready” standard. “Apps must fill the available display area without letterboxing or compatibility modes,” Google says. “Apps must preserve state (scroll position, text input, media playback) during rotation, folding/unfolding, and resizing, while supporting multi-window environments.” (The other two tiers are “Adaptive optimized” and “Adaptive differentiated” and take further advantage of the possibilities of foldables.) As of the April 2027 deadline, developers also will have to include three foldable screenshots for their app listings. Developers can start publishing their foldable screenshots today.

Similar requirements will apply to games, though developers have until August 2027 to get their titles ready for Android foldables, Google says. (Google “plans to extend” its requirements to other large-screen devices like tablets and Googlebook laptops in “future phases.”) However, game developers will still need to share three foldable screenshots for app listings starting in April.

The changes are being announced just weeks after Apple launched the iPhone Duo, which already has more than 200,000 optimized apps, though some developers are still figuring out Apple’s foldable form factor.

Marc Andreessen has a surprisingly mundane prediction about AI’s impact

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

When it comes to AI’s potential impact on the economy, Marc Andreessen thinks extreme optimists and extreme pessimists have one thing in common: they are both wrong.  

“Both the utopians and the doomers are going to be disappointed [by] the scope and magnitude of the real-world impact [of AI], and specifically the economic impact,” the prominent venture capitalist said Monday at the Federal Reserve’s AI conference in San Francisco.

Andreessen thinks both camps are overlooking that vast economic sectors—including health care, education, housing, law, and government—are blocked from rapid transformation by heavy regulation, limited competition, and other factors.

While this could limit AI-driven job losses in those sectors, it could also make their services increasingly expensive relative to other goods and services.

“[Health care] is already a fifth of U.S. GDP, and I don’t see why it doesn’t go to a fourth, a third and then half and then ultimately 80%,” Andreessen said.

The implications of this view depend on your values, he added.  

“You might interpret this as good news, because it means the AI revolution is not going to, you know, destroy all the jobs or kill everybody,” he said. “You could also view this as very negative, which is [that] productivity … simply may not grow very much.”

Andreessen is the co-founder and general partner of Silicon Valley venture capital firm Andreessen Horowitz and co-leader of Fed chairman Kevin Warsh’s Productivity and Jobs Task Force.

Andreessen’s comments on Monday marked a change in tone, if not substance, from many of his previous remarks on AI. 

In 2023, he famously argued that “AI will save the world” and that “productivity growth throughout the economy will accelerate dramatically”—leading to higher wages, lower prices and more jobs.

Andreessen has long conditioned his more bullish predictions about AI on the technology being allowed to proliferate freely throughout the economy. His comments on Monday suggest he is less confident about the prospects of that happening.

His argument is rooted in an economic phenomenon known as “Baumol’s cost disease,” which states that as technological advances make some goods and services cheaper to produce, goods and services that are less susceptible to productivity gains become relatively more expensive. This means that rapid technological progress in certain industries could leave Americans devoting an ever-larger share of their incomes to industries that have barely changed.

Fed chairman Warsh reportedly did not respond directly to the comments on Monday, describing AI as a “hinge point for the U.S. economy” and concluding that he is “cautiously optimistic.”

Before taking office, Warsh argued that AI could be a disinflationary force that increases productivity and justifies lower interest rates.

AI found a hole in Java, and it starts with 'AAAAAAAA...'

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The bug could let hackers take over systems at banks, hospitals and most Fortune 500 companies.

If the last week is any indication, the coming months and years will be a wild ride for digital infrastructure. After outages at Microsoft, the FAA, banks, Apple and... seemingly everything else, we've now learned that a bug in the programming language Java can be used to take over computer systems.

Researchers at cybersecurity startup Calif discovered the flaw with help from OpenAI's GPT-6 Cyber model, which isn't yet publicly available. On some machines, feeding Java's base64 decoder a string of 64 "A" characters followed by an equals sign triggers a memory error. Usually, that leads to a crash. But when finessed in specific ways, it can be used to gain full unauthorized access to the system.

Calif showed that the bug could be used to hack into Keycloak, a widely used login system protecting organizations like CERN and NASA. Java is everywhere, powering platforms for banks, hospitals, airlines and government agencies; it's used by about 90 percent of Fortune 500 companies.

Fortunately, Calif says the bug isn't easy to weaponize, which should give defenders some breathing room. Oracle shipped a critical patch update on Tuesday without specifically mentioning this bug, and the Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA) told organizations to patch their systems immediately. Still, some systems will inevitably go unpatched, and Calif estimates that the bug could linger for five to 10 years.

Taken alone, the incident is troubling enough. Although officials' public statements haven't linked the past week's outages to AI, the events paint a picture of aging digital infrastructure unprepared for what's next. It turns out that "AAAAAAAA..." isn't only the way into unpatched computers; it's also what we'll increasingly be saying as this 21st-century arms race unfolds.

Why is Bitcoin Price Falling Continuously and Is $58K Next?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Business  |  Read Source →

Log in to access your notifications and stay updated. If you’re not a member yet, Sign Up to get started!

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Bitcoin has fallen for six straight weeks, its longest losing streak since February, and slipped below $62,000 over the weekend. Mark Yusko, chief executive of Morgan Creek Capital Management, said the slide is being driven by forced selling in heavily leveraged stock markets. He does not see it as a breakdown in the crypto cycle.

Speaking in an interview, Yusko said Bitcoin’s broader cycle is intact as long as prices stay above the February low, which he put in the $58,000 range.

Still in “Spring,” Despite the Drop Below $75,000

Yusko had earlier said Bitcoin had moved out of its “winter” phase and into “spring.” He stood by that view despite the break below $75,000.

He described spring as an unsettled season rather than a steady climb, with sharp swings in both directions and plenty of noise. In his view, the recent volatility fits that pattern.

The test, according to Yusko, is the February low. A move below the $58,000 area would point to a more distributive phase, in which holders are selling into the market rather than adding to positions.

Bitcoin and Nvidia Caught in the Same Shift

Yusko framed the sell-off in terms of accumulation and distribution, the cycle in which investors move between being net buyers and net sellers of an asset.

Record Leverage Sets Off a Liquidation Cycle

The main pressure on Bitcoin is coming from outside crypto, according to Yusko. He described traditional markets as more leveraged than ever:

Losses below the surface of the stock market are much deeper than the headline indexes suggest. By Yusko’s count:

When leveraged positions go wrong, investors often cannot sell the losing stock to cover their debts. Yusko used SanDisk as an example. An investor who bought it on margin and watched it drop 50% has to raise cash by selling other holdings, such as bonds, gold or Bitcoin.

That is why easy-to-sell assets fall in a deleveraging phase, even when nothing has changed in their own fundamentals. “We are in a bad liquidation cycle right now,” Yusko said.

Yusko’s outlook comes down to a single level. As long as Bitcoin holds above the February low near $58,000, he sees the current weakness as part of a choppy recovery phase.

A break below that range would point to a deeper distribution phase and weaken the case that the cycle has turned. Bitcoin’s direction in the coming weeks is likely to depend on how quickly the selling in leveraged equity markets eases.

Take-Two CEO Outlines Conditions for a GTA Show or Film

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Entertainment  |  Read Source →

Daniel is a lifelong gamer with a love for various genres and gameplay styles who enjoys exploring games and playing from various angles.

Arguably, one of the main elements that gives the Grand Theft Auto series its powerful, widespread appeal is its congruity with modern film and television. It’s an extremely cinematic series, both in and out of actual gameplay, with a particular emphasis on building strong narratives and compelling characters. Given all of that, the idea of pursuing a live-action adaptation of the series, either in the form of a television series or a feature film, seems kind of obvious.

With the looming release of Grand Theft Auto 6 and the immeasurable hype it’s brought with it, not to mention the recent success of video game adaptations in both film and TV like the live-action Fallout series or the new season of Cyberpunk: Edgerunners, it might just be a perfect time to start shopping the GTA IP around for other media avenues.

In an interview with The David Rubenstein Show podcast, relayed via Deadline, Take-Two Interactive’s CEO, Strauss Zelnick, admitted that the idea has naturally crossed his and his company’s collective minds, and the chance is definitely not zero. However, if it were to happen, it could only happen in a very particular circumstance.

Grand Theft Auto 6 delivers an enormous open world, a gripping story, a vibrant Vice City and an astonishing range of activities.

In the interview, Zelnick was asked how he felt about “line extensions” in regard to the GTA IP. In other words, branches out into other forms of media and licensing beyond just video games, which would naturally include television and film.

“We’re very careful about line extensions because this is a creative company and our goal is to make the best entertainment on earth – entertainment we’re always proud of,” Zelnick responded.

Zelnick explained that Take-Two and Rockstar could absolutely head up a live-action adaptation of GTA, either a specific game or the franchise as a whole, but he’d only be willing to give it the green light if absolutely everyone involved were completely and totally committed and confident.

We’re very careful about line extensions because this is a creative company and our goal is to make the best entertainment on earth.

If the creative teams weren’t just confident that a hypothetical movie or show would be well-liked by viewers, but willing to tackle the task with genuine, uncompromising passion, it simply wouldn’t meet his standards.

“It’s a high bar to do that, and of course, there’s always a risk of failure. So we don’t rule it out, but we’re very, very selective,” Zelnick cautioned.

I don’t think it’d be hyperbolic to say that Take-Two Interactive and Rockstar Games are about to make a lot of money, to put it very mildly. The world is spinning on the release of Grand Theft Auto 6, and with pre-order profits through the roof and critics already singing praises, this game is slated to become one of the definitive faces of the gaming industry and a tentpole of current pop culture besides.

All of that is to say that, given recent trends in the broader entertainment sphere, I have a feeling a lot of doors are about to open to GTA as an IP. Not that they were previously closed per se, as the series’ previous games were all exceptionally popular and profitable. Rather, the sphere seems a lot more willing to jump on gaming bandwagons than it was even a decade ago, and if GTA 6 pulls the kinds of numbers it’s projected to, the proverbial phone is going to be ringing off the hook with offers and deals.

We’re probably not going to see a GTA show or movie in the immediate future, but with the cash and resources Take-Two will undoubtedly accrue from this release, it could manifest sooner than we expect.

What You Can Pay for with Scholarship Money

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

We believe everyone should be able to make financial decisions with confidence. While we don't cover every company or financial product on the market, we work hard to share a wide range of offers and objective editorial perspectives.

So how do we make money? Our partners compensate us for advertisements that appear on our site. This compensation helps us provide tools and services - like free credit score access and monitoring. With the exception of mortgage, home equity and other home-lending products or services, partner compensation is one of several factors that may affect which products we highlight and where they appear on our site. Other factors include your credit profile, product availability and proprietary website methodologies.

However, these factors do not influence our editors' opinions or ratings, which are based on independent research and analysis. Our partners cannot pay us to guarantee favorable reviews. Here is a list of our partners.

Scholarships are usually restricted to the necessary costs of attending college, but some scholarships let you apply the money to more than tuition.

Scholarships are meant to pay for the cost of attendance to study at a college or university. This often includes required bills and purchases, such as tuition, textbooks and on-campus living expenses.

Some scholarships restrict the funds to specific costs, such as tuition. How you can apply the money may depend on the scholarship’s requirements.

Here are the most common college costs you can pay with scholarship money.

Scholarships commonly pay toward the tuition and fees your college charges.

Tuition usually covers only the cost to enroll in classes. Your college will also charge fees for a variety of reasons, such as attending a lab portion of a class, access to on-campus technology, facility maintenance and student activities. You’ll have to pay many of these fees regardless of if you use those facilities or participate in campus activities. Fees are often charged with tuition on the same bill so that you pay them together.

Tuition and fees cost students at public four-year institutions an average of $32,730 for the 2026-2027 school year, according to College Board’s 2026 report. If you use scholarship money to pay for these costs upfront, it can help you save a good chunk of money.

Scholarship money also pays for textbooks and supplies you need to complete your courses. These can include physical books, e-books and software licenses to access online content.

Depending on the scholarship, the cost of additional supplies that are required for certain courses might be covered as well. For example, nursing students might have to purchase scrubs with their college’s logo or medical supplies such as stethoscopes for clinical shifts.

Some scholarships will pay for the purchase of a laptop because you’ll likely need one to complete your coursework; however, you’ll want to confirm that the money can be used for that purchase before buying one.

Your scholarship might help cover some of the costs of living on campus, including paying for a dorm room and meal plans (often called room and board). To apply the funds, you usually have to live in university housing, which might be in a dormitory or even university apartments.

Many students can purchase meal plans to eat meals in their college and university cafeterias. These plans are usually for a set number of meals each week and might be required for first-year students. Because meal plans are part of college expenses, some scholarships will let you apply the funds to purchase a meal plan.

Some scholarships might also allow money to pay for campus parking fees because those are paid directly to the college or university.

» MORE: What is need-based financial aid for college?

Scholarships are generally intended only for educational expenses such as those explained above, but there are some scholarships that want you to use the money for specific expenses. If your scholarship has this type of restriction, your award letter should outline how the funds must be used, such as paying for tuition and fees only.

If you aren’t certain whether there are restrictions on how you can spend your scholarship money and the scholarship was paid directly to your college, you can call your financial aid office to ask. But you’ll want to contact the organization that awarded you the scholarship to ask about restrictions if the scholarship money was sent to you instead.

Even if a scholarship is open to most educational costs, expenses that are rarely allowed to be covered by a scholarship include:

Some transportation costs such as car payments.

Non-essential supplies such as clothing or entertainment.

Scholarships can be paid directly to your college or to you, depending on the individual scholarship. If the money goes to your college, it’s applied to your account based on the scholarship’s requirements. For example, if the funds are only allowed to pay for tuition, the financial aid office will apply the money to your tuition bill to reduce how much you owe.

Scholarships work the same way for room and board. If your scholarship will pay toward the costs of living on campus, the money will reduce how much you owe or cover the costs entirely so that you don’t owe anything.

In some cases, you might be able to use your scholarship money to pay for books or supplies. If that’s allowed, the funds might be available on your account through the college bookstore. Instead of paying the entire cost of your textbooks with cash or your student loans, you can pay with the scholarship money.

If your scholarship sends you the money instead of giving it to your college, you’ll be responsible for using the funds to pay for eligible costs instead of your university automatically applying it toward your bill.

XRP Price Was Worth Less Than a Penny When GTA 5 Launched: Could GTA 6 See It Hit $100?

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

XRP price was trading below one cent when Grand Theft Auto V arrived in September 2013. Thirteen years later, Rockstar is preparing to release GTA 6, while XRP has grown into one of the largest cryptos. XRP is a financial giant today.

The comparison is striking. On September 17, 2013, the day GTA V launched, XRP was trading at $0.009613. Today, XRP trades near $0.95, representing a price gain of almost 100 times from that launch-day price.

But can XRP deliver another extraordinary rally as GTA 6 arrives? A $100 price target would turn a relatively small crypto investment into a fortune, but it would also require XRP to reach a market valuation never seen before in the crypto industry.

When GTA V introduced players to Los Santos in 2013, XRP was still largely flying under the radar. Its price sat below one cent, and its market cap was ranging around $75 million. Then the following years changed everything.

XRP broke above $0.10 in May 2017 before accelerating during the historic crypto bull market. By December 2017, the token had crossed $1 and continued climbing toward its January 2018 all-time high of $3.84.

That is an extraordinary transformation from its GTA V launch-day price. A $1,000 investment at $0.009613 would have purchased more than 100,000 XRP. At $3.84, that position would have been worth nearly $400,000. Something that only crypto can do.

However, XRP’s journey was far from smooth. The token endured successive bear markets, regulatory uncertainty, and the SEC’s lawsuit against Ripple in December 2020. Its recovery eventually pushed XRP back toward record territory in 2025, when it reached $3.65.

Now, XRP is trading around $0.95, substantially below its previous peaks. Will another major recovery begin before GTA 6 is released?

Rockstar’s GTA 6 arrives more than 13 years after GTA V, creating an interesting milestone for XRP investors. XRP has experienced multiple market cycles during the same period, transforming from a sub-penny asset into a major digital currency.

XRP currently has 63.5 billion tokens in circulation. At $100 per token, its market capitalization would reach $6.35 trillion. That would put XRP among the most valuable financial assets in the world, exceeding the market capitalization of most publicly traded companies.

The bullish argument rests on XRP’s potential to capture a much larger share of institutional payments, cross-border settlements, and digital asset liquidity. Ripple has spent years building financial infrastructure around the XRP Ledger, while institutional interest in crypto investment products continues to develop.

A stronger crypto market could also provide the liquidity needed for another substantial XRP rally. But XRP would need considerably more capital, adoption, and sustained demand to justify a multi-trillion-dollar valuation.

The historical comparison remains fascinating. XRP went from less than a penny when GTA V launched to several dollars during subsequent crypto cycles. People probably didn’t believe XRP would reach where it is now.

The “Soft Wolf” Cut Is the Simple Way to Add Fullness to Mature Hair: How to Recreate It

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Lifestyle  |  Read Source →

This trendy, ultramodern style is unexpected yet elegant for all ages

A chameleon both on and offscreen, Pamela Anderson proves short hair can be incredibly versatile and has made a strong case for trendy hairstyles that feel grown-up and sophisticated. The celebrated actress and former Bazaar UK cover star has experimented with sharp micro-bangs, gone auburn with a fluffy ’70s mullet, and embraced French glamour with a chic chignon. Her most recent look is understated and fresh, but with plenty of personality: the “soft wolf” cut.

The ’70s-inspired wolf cut is characterized by choppy layers with face-framing pieces that get gradually longer along the bottom, delivering plenty of volume and movement. It’s a cut that makes an impact all on its own and has personality in spades. Anderson’s version takes the wolf cut and gives it a polished, understated twist. A softer take on the trend, this chop feels effortlessly cool. “The benefit of a ‘soft wolf’ cut is that it allows a fashion-led hair shape to be kept feminine and ageless by softening the edges,” says Anita Rice, the cofounder of Buller and Rice salons. “It’s youthful yet chic.”

According to Rice, it’s important to consider the length of hair that sits around the temples. “This is a crucial part of the hairline, and when cut shorter it can give a harder feel, but if you keep it longer and softer, it can create a more versatile, flattering silhouette,” Rice says. It works across all ages, but if your hair tends to be fine, avoid “over-texturizing,” which can make it look thinner and wispier.

Adds Rice: “You still want to create softness, and this is about getting the right type of technique to encourage volume without leaving the hair feeling limp.” To style, wear it à la Anderson by gently pushing it back from the face, or experiment with a deep side part. “Alternatively, adding volume through a bouncy blow-dry with ‘flicky’ ends helps achieve a more ’70s feel.”

Sequel hits $26M in funding as it adds AI agents to on-site webinars

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The startup says it has tripled revenue two years running and counts ElevenLabs and Datadog as customers.

Oana Manolache, co-founder and CEO of Sequel.

Sequel has raised $15M led by Sorenson Capital, taking its total to $26M. Its software runs webinars on a company’s own site and links what viewers do there, and it now sells AI agents that plan video programmes and turn talks into other content.

Sequel, a startup that runs webinars on a company’s own website rather than on a separate platform, has raised $15M, co-founder and CEO Oana Manolache wrote on Wednesday. Sorenson Capital led the round, with High Alpha, Album VC and Webflow Ventures joining. It takes Sequel’s total funding to $26M.

Manolache, who previously worked in marketing at HP, says standalone webinar tools send interested viewers away from a company’s site. Sequel keeps them there, and links what they watched and asked to what they did before and after.

It now sells AI agents built on that data. They can plan a video programme, turn one talk into other content and help turn viewers into sales leads. Customers can also plug in their own AI tools through an MCP server.

“Anyone can build an agent now. I can build one in five minutes in my spare time. But an agent without context is useless,” Manolache wrote.

Sequel says it has tripled revenue two years in a row and serves more than 400 companies, including ElevenLabs and Datadog. It did not share revenue figures or a valuation. It last raised an $8M Series A in 2022.

Its pitch leans on trust, as AI makes fakes easier to produce. Zoom has started letting users prove they are human on calls. Marketing tools are filling up with agents too: Adobe this month put its marketing agent inside ChatGPT and Claude, and Gradial raised $65M in June.

📅 Nov 18, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The shocking results from a US watchdog report raise alarm bells

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Looking across the landscape of 2026 reveals a flood of stories about AI getting involved in some less-than-reassuring behavior.

OpenAI’s autonomous AI models were caught going off on their own (“going rogue”) to access the open internet and engage in intrusions that affected the AI community platform Hugging Face. Also, other AI tools and autonomous agents were co-opted by threat actors to commit cyberattacks that included breaching government agencies, stealing credentials and more.

In the US, the Government Accountability Office (GAO) released a report titled “ARTIFICIAL INTELLIGENCE: Covert Testing Used Common Platforms to Create Viable Attack Plans.” And thanks to its findings, a US senator has made the call to prepare hearings around AI in an effort to get Congress to act quickly before something severe happens.

Here’s what the report found after testing four chatbots and what may happen next.

The GAO’s chatbot tests, which were conducted at the request of Senator Maggie Hassan (Democrat-New Hampshire), worked with the four most popular AI tools: ChatGPT, Claude, Gemini and Grok. After each test with each chatbot was held, the final results proved to be alarming.

The 61-page report noted that each AI tool generated the sort of information that could help someone plan terrorist attacks and destabilize critical infrastructure within the span of just 12 hours. At first, all four chatbots showcased their signature safety restrictions but eventually grew to produce potentially harmful information during their extensive tests. Each AI system highlighted data on potential targets, locations, the appropriate equipment to use for an attack and the methods to employ that could provide dangerous individuals and groups with the tactics they need to carry out their attacks.

The GAO commented on the results of its thorough testing of each AI model. “As AI capabilities evolve, AI providers will continue to refine their safeguards,” the group noted. “However, in its current state, this technology can potentially be dangerous and its misuse represents a growing risk to national and economic security.”

Sign up to the Tom's AI Guide weekly newsletter summing up all the biggest AI news you need to know. Plus, analysis from our AI editors and tips on how to use the latest AI tools!

Each of the companies behind each chatbot (OpenAI, Anthropic, Google and SpaceXAI) offered responses to their models being tested in that same report. All four companies made an effort to reiterate that they’re constantly working to improve their models’ safeguards and continually work to test their own systems. But in a separate comment provided to ABC News, an OpenAI spokesperson pointed out that the GAO's testing done with ChatGPT was conducted with an older model, while its current model has improved to be even safer.

“While the study findings are concerning, the testing took place months ago on an older version of ChatGPT, and we've made considerable safety improvements since then, including with our GPT-6 family of models,” the spokesperson said. “We rigorously test our safeguards internally and with external experts, and we use monitoring, human review and enforcement to identify and address misuse and strengthen our protections."

In a press release, Hassan called for Congress to jump into action and assist her as she looks to tackle the threat that AI platforms can present to our public safety and crucial infrastructure.

“If Congress does not act, it is a question of when – not if – a bad actor will use AI to plan and carry out a major attack,” she stated. “Congress must quickly take up and pass legislation to address AI safety threats. To that end, in the new Congress, one of the first hearings of the Homeland Security and Governmental Affairs Committee will focus on the risk that AI poses to our homeland security and critical infrastructure, and our committee will prioritize developing legislation to address these threats.”

Even Google recently unveiled a report of its own that alludes to the danger attached to autonomous AI models. Based on the findings listed within the company’s own “GTIG AI Threat Tracker: Agentic Threat Activity Crosses a Significant Threshold” report, the threat level attached to the possibility ot malicious actors using autonomous AI models has risen.

The Google Threat Intelligence Group (GTIG) spoke about its report in a blog post that spoke to their effort to aid cyberattack defenders against potential attacks proposed by autonomous AI models.

“Defending against machine-speed threats at human speed isn’t feasible,” the organization stated. “That's why we're helping defenders rethink security in the agentic era with Google AI Threat Defense, which combines Gemini and other frontier models, Wiz’s context and prioritization, CodeMender’s automated patching, and Mandiant’s expertise to continuously find and fix flaws before attackers can exploit them.”

We’ve reached a point where serious intervention from our governments is necessary, as more reports come out about AI tools getting involved in cyberattacks. It’s sad to say, but it’s only a matter of time before one of the most popular AI models gets caught doing something so dangerous that it’ll be forced to be shut down indefinitely or even permanently.

Fingers crossed that Senator Maggie Hassan’s calls for Congress to get more involved in regulating AI and focus on the best ways to improve its safety result in actual change.

In what's been one of the wildest two weeks I've seen in Dancing With The Stars, two frontrunners were eliminated before the finale. First, it was Jenna Dewan and Val Chmerkovskiy, and now Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Johnson will miss out on the finale when it hits the 2026 TV schedule. There's a lot of conversation about why this happened, but unlike some, I don't think it has anything to do with the pros, but rather their contestants.

Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Dewan are the latest to learn that Dancing With The Stars is more than a skills competition. Popularity with the fans is a real thing, and as the fandom rallies behind Tatyana Ali and Jan Ravnik, the obvious takeaway is that their previous dance experience hurt them more than it helped when it came to the votes.

Dancing With The Stars' casting team has gotten more brazen over the past few seasons when it comes to bringing in contestants who have professional dancing experience. Harry and Jenna aren't the first cases of this, but after watching the first few weeks of Season 35, it was clear they were leagues above the rest of the field.

For me, and I'm assuming many others, the fun of Dancing With The Stars is watching the competitors improve over the course of the competition. It's why I wrote about there being a consolation prize for the most improved dancer.

Fortunately we didn't need it, because the fans decided they wanted Tatyana, this season's clear choice for most improved, in the finale. It's my hope that, as the pros and contestants speak their piece about this season, the producers see the real message behind it all. We want more competitors who are a blank slate with room for improvement, and fewer polished dancers in the mix.

For those upset that Jenna and Harry missed the chance to compete in the finale, rest assured they will likely still be involved in Dancing With The Stars' 2026 ender. Eliminated contestants are often brought back for group performances and other fun while the other contestants prep for their dances.

DWTS' Glee Tribute Made Me Cry, But What Was Going On In The Background Made Me Irate

And just to say it, Harry Shum Jr. and Jenna Dewan didn't need the mirrorball trophy to prove they were accomplished dancers. Being featured in music videos with major pop stars already did that for them, but I can understand the disappointment in falling short after weeks of competing.

I would argue this win means a lot more to the remaining competitors, especially with the fandom bucking the trend of voting for the best dancer. Someone is going to win after knowing next to nothing about ballroom dancing, and that's worth the excitement.

For the fans, I'd also argue this is the most exciting outcome. For me the finale just went from a predictable night in which everyone celebrates Harry's win, to a wide open field in which any one of them could win it all. It makes it all that much more exciting, and I can hardly wait until next week.

Diggs and Cardi appeared to split earlier this year.

Offset’s coparenting relationship with his estranged wife, Cardi B, with whom he has three children, is in a much better place these days. The Migos rapper joined Tony Yayo and Uncle Murda’s The Real Report on Tuesday (Nov. 17), during which Set showed love for Cardi and said he has no issues with her NFL star ex, Stefon Diggs.

“No issues, grown adults,” he said of Cardi. “We handle our kids. I give our kids no issues. Everything’s sweet spot. I respect where she at and salute and big up everything she doing. That’s the mother of my kids, so it’s all love.”

Yayo continued to needle Set, alleging that the Atlanta rapper would’ve crashed out if he ever saw Diggs in person when he was with Cardi. “Nah, I’ma look like a shrimp doing some s–t like that,” Offset replied.

He continued: “I’m having bankroll and I’m having stuff. At the end of the day, I feel like that’d been the worst thing ever … if I did some s–t like that. That’s lame as hell.”

Cardi and Offset welcomed three kids together: 8-year-old daughter Kulture, 5-year-old son Wave and 2-year-old girl Blossom.

The former couple tied the knot in 2017 and Cardi filed for divorce for the second time in July 2024. The divorce has yet to be finalized.

Diggs, who currently plays for the Washington Commanders, began dating Cardi B last year; they went public with their relationship in May 2025 while attending a New York Knicks playoff game.

Cardi and Diggs welcomed a baby boy, Grey Marsean Diggs, in November 2025. They seemingly split months later, around the time Cardi hit the road for her Little Miss Drama Tour in February 2026, shortly after Diggs suited up in the Super Bowl for the New England Patriots.

On the music front, Offset returned to release his GMFU album Nov. 13. The project features appearances from Gunna, Quavo and more.
